incidence-space ledger.  No unrecorded $p>2$ moment and no intersection of
unrelated label laws is used.  The ledger is not assumed again in the main
theorem.
The positive initial endpoint scale is included through
$S_\lambda^{a_{\rm scl}}$.  Its excess is then split off by the
\emph{tilted} estimate \eqref{eq:upper-scale-excess-tail}, on that same
weighted complete law, before the phase estimate is applied.  Context,
stopping, buffer, gate, continuation, and PPE losses are separately charged
by $\mathrm{TENERGY}_{W}$ on this identical tilt, while each additional
bad-support class is charged through $\mathrm{CLOCK\_PROB}_{W}$ and every
additive occurrence through $\mathrm{CLOCK\_SRC}_{W}$.  For cemetery pieces the
passage from weighted probability to actual discarded current is exactly
\eqref{eq:cemetery-current-weighted-bound}; it is not inferred from the
flux sum.
The occurrence-level disintegration
\eqref{eq:physical-occurrence-envelope-disintegration} prevents a component
cost from being appended after the tree moment.  The exact
physical-to-source identification is \eqref{eq:prop-q-exact-m-match}
together with its compatibility graph, and its envelope domination is precisely
\eqref{eq:propagated-q-physical-match}; none of the moment arguments in this
proof is claimed to derive either statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional pathwise quantitative CM2 criterion]
\label{thm:global-cm2-assembly}
Let an analytic-boundary or circular deformation $a(s)$ satisfy
$\mathrm{QT}_{\rm path}$ and the gauge-compatibility restriction
\eqref{eq:constant-component-mass-vector}.
Assume a compact finite-horizon chart and, along every segment
$a(\theta s)$ and every $L\le L(s)$, one spacetime refinement satisfying
$\mathrm{UCR}_{\rm sh}$, with:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(H\arabic*)}]
\item all hypotheses of
      Lemma~\ref{lem:measurable-refinement-moments} hold on the same complete
      actual/split tree.  This explicitly includes the normalized refinement
      kernels \eqref{eq:refinement-kernel-mass}, the weight normalization
      \eqref{eq:full-product-weight-normalization}, the pointwise Mark--$Z$
      domination \eqref{eq:full-mark-cemetery-domination}, the full cemetery
      product envelope \eqref{eq:full-cemetery-product-envelope}, all
      conditional moments, and the genuine complete-tree cemetery moment;
      no collection of separate marginal moments is substituted for this
      package.  In addition assume the finite-horizon functional-correlation bound of \cite{LS}, the
      standard one-state marked quotient, and exponential return/context
      moments; a dominant inverse-scale moment
      $\sum_\lambda M_\lambda
      \mathfrak d_\lambda^{A_{\rm dom}q_D}<\infty$ and a positive initial-scale
      moment $\sum_\lambda M_\lambda
      S_\lambda^{a_{\rm scl}q_S}<\infty$; the separate terminal-law
      recovery input $\mathrm{REC}(q_{\rm rec})$, with
      $q_{\rm rec},\epsilon,\eta_{\rm lo}$ chosen to satisfy
      \eqref{eq:delayed-recovery-eta-window}; and
      exponents $q_H,q_F,q_D,q_S,q_R,q_C$ satisfying
      \eqref{eq:holder-conjugate-margin}, with $p_W$ chosen in
      \eqref{eq:available-full-product-pW}; the refinement moments hold
      conditionally along the common refinement as in
      \eqref{eq:conditional-refinement-moments}, whereas the possibly
      unbounded cemetery factor satisfies the genuine joint law
      \eqref{eq:complete-tree-cemetery-moment} (or the earlier-parent kernel
      version \eqref{eq:parent-kernel-cemetery-moment});
\item forward/reverse proper-family growth and coupling uniformly for the
      compact slowly moving table class, in the form of Theorems~1'--2' of
      \cite{SYZ}; Lemma~\ref{lem:signed-observable-multiplier} then supplies
      the signed multiplier bridge;
\item On the persistent clean gate assume
      \[
      \begin{gathered}
       \mathrm{NST}_{\rm phys},\ \mathrm{SS}(d_Z),\
       \mathrm{ACTUAL\_SLOPE\_BRIDGE},\\
       \mathrm{PAIR\_SAFE\_REFERENCE},\ \mathrm{PPE}_{N}(d_Z),
      \end{gathered}
      \]
      together with
      \[
       \begin{gathered}
       \mathrm{CEM\_DOM},\ \mathrm{TENERGY}_{W},\\
       \mathrm{DUAL\_MASS\_KERNEL},\ \mathrm{MPD},\
       \mathrm{POST\_CARRIER\_DOM},\ \mathrm{PHYS\_TEST\_LIFT},\\
       \mathrm{PHYS\_TREE\_MATCH},\ \mathrm{PROP\_Q\_MATCH},\\
       \mathrm{MAIN\_CLOCK\_COVERAGE},\
       \mathrm{SEL\_JSL}_{p_{\rm joint}},\\
       \mathrm{CLOCK\_PROB}_{W},\ \mathrm{CLOCK\_SRC}_{W},\
       \mathrm{KRH}_{p_W}
       \end{gathered}
      \]
      \[
       \mathrm{USC}_{\rm end}^{W}(q_g^W),\ \mathrm{BR}_{\rm sec}.
      \]
      In particular, the occurrence of
      $\mathrm{PAIR\_SAFE\_REFERENCE}$ in this hypothesis includes, on
      every actual frozen parent at which
      Proposition~\ref{prop:ppe-implies-pair-tenergy} is called, the uniform
      survivor normalization
      \begin{equation}
      \label{eq:global-parentwise-survivor-floor}
       M_{\mathsf G}^{W}(\eta)\ge\frac12.
      \end{equation}
      For a parent with bounded $N_{\rm time}$ this is an independent
      requirement.  The clock estimate
      $\mathrm{CLOCK\_PROB}_{W}$ by itself does not imply it.
      The marked kernel satisfies the global finite-envelope closure
      \eqref{eq:global-finite-marked-envelope}, the exhaustive owner identity
      \eqref{eq:exhaustive-source-owner-partition}, the resolution coverage
      \eqref{eq:nonmain-clock-coverage}--
      \eqref{eq:main-clock-coverage}, the exact product reference
      \eqref{eq:unselected-reference-law}--
      \eqref{eq:reference-slope-identification},
      the bounded bilinear physical-test lift and its exact commutation
      \eqref{eq:bounded-physical-test-lift}--
      \eqref{eq:physical-test-lift-commutation}, the physical/tree identity
      \eqref{eq:physical-tree-RN-identity}, and the record-preserving
      physical/source bridge
      \eqref{eq:physical-occurrence-m-measure}--
      \eqref{eq:propagated-q-physical-match}.  It also satisfies the
      projective consistency
      \eqref{eq:source-kernel-projective-consistency} and the main-source
      likelihood/base
      bounds \eqref{eq:selection-safe-likelihood-rates} and
      \eqref{eq:main-source-base-mass} uniformly on the same parent cells.
      Proposition~\ref{prop:ppe-implies-pair-tenergy} then supplies
      \eqref{eq:pair-tenergy-bound} on this same law.  For every nonempty main time--Jensen cell the unselected reference
      energy \eqref{eq:unselected-time-reference-energy} holds uniformly on
      those same frozen parents.
      The selection-safe joint source likelihood is verified either by the
      same-carrier clause of Lemma~\ref{lem:joint-source-factor-criterion} or
      by its explicit $\mathrm{CARRIER\_MATCH}$ alternative.
      Here \(\mathrm{KRH}_{p_W}\) is uniform on every frozen parent kernel.
      These interfaces and the dominant chart bounds
      \eqref{eq:primitive-dominant-chart-bounds} have the
      parameter-uniform constants required by $\mathrm{UCR}_{\rm sh}$ for
      $C_{\rm mid}(\eta_{\rm hi})N\le L(s)$.  The cutoffs
      \eqref{eq:three-frequency-cutoffs} satisfy
      $0<\eta_{\rm lo}<\eta_{\rm hi}$ and
      $r_{\rm obs}>2+\beta_{\rm phase}^{W}$,
      \eqref{eq:ultra-high-exponent-window}, and the complete phase exponent
      \eqref{eq:complete-phase-exponent}; longer shallow times are not estimated
      by this interface;
\item the two-chart nondegeneracy $|\nabla G|\ge c_G$ and
      $\mathrm{FZ}_{\rm geom}(\theta_Z)$, including the full tangent coarea identity,
      weighted component complexity, angle sublevel, and good-angle exponent
      $A_{\rm ang}$, together with $\mathrm{SF}_{\rm split}$ and
      $\mathrm{CP}_{\rm env}$ on the same spacetime cells;
      Proposition~\ref{prop:envelope-dynamical-tube}
      then supplies \eqref{eq:FZ-dynamical-tube}; the shallow finite-$s$
      source satisfies $\mathrm{SHCOL}_{\rm env}$ and the strict nonempty
      window \eqref{eq:shallow-time-window}; the terminal tail satisfies
      $\mathrm{TAIL}_{\rm env}^{+}$ and
      $\mathrm{NREC}_{\rm deep}(q_{\rm deep})$ with
      $q_{\rm deep}>\max\{\alpha_+,\alpha_-\}$;
\item $\mathrm{MT}_{\rm DQ}$ holds for the fixed-gauge operator quotient,
      including its exact typed decomposition and
      \eqref{eq:uniform-gauge-DQ-regular}--
      \eqref{eq:fixed-time-evolution-convergence}.  The regular defect
      satisfies, for some
      $1\le R\le3$,
      \[
       \sum_\chi\|\zeta_\chi k_{\rm reg}\|_{W^{R,1}}<\infty.
      \]
      The local face amplitudes have the scaled
      $\mathcal A^\alpha$ regularity used in
      Proposition~\ref{prop:scaled-face-density}; observable regularity is
      above the dominant two-dimensional Fourier threshold.  The joint face
      part of \eqref{eq:event-physical-norm} is concluded from
      Proposition~\ref{prop:global-labelled-ledger}, not assumed here.
      The growing-depth face assembly satisfies
      $\mathrm{FACE}_{\rm 2CUT}$, in particular the no-$|s|^{-1}$ middle
      estimate \eqref{eq:face-middle-tail}.  Independently, all main and
      nonmain face/word owners satisfy
      either $\mathrm{FACE\_TIME}_{\rm CM2}$ or
      $\mathrm{FACE\_TIME}_{\rm REC}$ on the same propagated
      $q$-restriction.
\end{enumerate}
Then the complete double-centered fixed-gauge first operator defect satisfies
\[
 |\langle Q^nK_VQ^mg,f\rangle|
 \le C\tau^m\vartheta^n\|g\|_{C^r}\|f\|_{C^r}.
\]
The assembled difference quotients converge after the independent word-depth
and time-depth splits.  At every fixed $(m,n)$ the response,
product-current, regular, and face blocks converge respectively by
\eqref{eq:strong-DQ-convergence},
\eqref{eq:product-current-DQ-convergence},
\eqref{eq:regular-DQ-weak-convergence}, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:face-two-cutoff}.  The shallow long-time part converges to zero by
Theorem~\ref{thm:shallow-two-threshold}, and the deep remainder does so by
Theorem~\ref{thm:time-depth-diagonal}.  More precisely, for centered
$g,f\in C^r(M)$ define the complete arrays
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:complete-CM2-DQ-arrays}
 B_s(m,n):=\langle Q_s^nK_s^{\rm DQ}Q_s^mg,f\rangle,
 \qquad
 B_0(m,n):=\langle Q^nK_VQ^mg,f\rangle .
\end{equation}
Then the complete quotient, including $E_s$, satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:complete-CM2-l1-convergence}
 \sum_{m,n\ge0}|B_s(m,n)-B_0(m,n)|\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
In particular, the $s\to0$ limit may be exchanged with the absolute CM2
double sum; this conclusion is stronger than termwise convergence.  More
precisely, if $c_{\rm off}$ is the off-diagonal coupling exponent and $c_Z$
is the exponent obtained by balancing the $\mathrm{FZ}_{\rm geom}$ angle sublevel, one
may choose
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:final-CM2-exponent}
 c_*<\min\left\{
 \begin{gathered}
 c_{\rm off},c_{\rm low},-2\log\theta_-,-2\log\theta_+,\\
 \eta_{\rm lo}\beta_{\rm phase}^{W},
 \eta_{\rm hi}\rho_\infty-a_{\rm ultra},c_{\rm scl},\\
 c_{\rm time,J}^{\rm val},c_{\rm time,TV}^{\rm src},
 \eta_{\rm lo}R,c_{\rm face,time},c_{\rm tail}-A_{\rm loss},c_Z
 \end{gathered}
 \right\}
\end{equation}
and then take $\tau=\vartheta=e^{-c_*/2}$ after changing the constant.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-gauge-centering} puts the limiting operator and every
finite-$s$ operator quotient under the same $\Pi_0$.  Coordinate
commutators remain in the response block, and their uniform product decay is
derived from \eqref{eq:strong-side-memory}--\eqref{eq:response-side-memory},
while \eqref{eq:strong-DQ-convergence}--
\eqref{eq:fixed-time-evolution-convergence} gives each fixed two-time limit; no
transported observable changes the target.  The mixed marginal/rank-one
terms remain in the fourth product-current type and form the exact centered
cluster \eqref{eq:typed-face-product-cluster} with their graph face.
They obey \eqref{eq:product-current-type} and vanish only against the two individually
centered endpoint tests; they are not absorbed into the regular or face
norm.  Every graph-supported
commutator boundary remains in the physical face atlas under \textup{(H5)}.
The regular fixed-term limit is
\eqref{eq:regular-DQ-weak-convergence}.  The face fixed-core limit follows
from \eqref{eq:face-DQ-weak-convergence} and the boundary-neighbourhood
estimate \eqref{eq:face-DQ-boundary-tightness};
Theorem~\ref{thm:face-two-cutoff} then removes the intermediate triangular
band before the terminal deep remainder is invoked.  Its uniform CM2
majorant is instead supplied, for every fixed core and every other
face/word owner, by Lemma~\ref{lem:face-time-majorant}; the event/section
ledgers alone are not used to infer time decay.
Thus no
uniform norm bound is used as a substitute for DQ convergence in either
typed block.
For the finite quotients, Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-gauge-centering} gives
\eqref{eq:typed-l1-DQ-convergence} on every fixed/shallow owner, with the
response type containing $E_s$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:shallow-two-threshold}
removes the remaining shallow long-time array in $\ell^1(\mathbb N^2)$,
and Theorem~\ref{thm:time-depth-diagonal} removes the complete deep array in
the same norm.  The triangle inequality now gives
\eqref{eq:complete-CM2-l1-convergence}; a pointwise or moving-spike argument
is not being used.
Proposition~\ref{prop:ppe-implies-pair-tenergy} first supplies
$\mathrm{PAIR\_TENERGY}$ on the exact unselected product law.
Proposition~\ref{prop:global-labelled-ledger} then supplies the complete face
part of \eqref{eq:event-physical-norm} from the primitive moment hypotheses.
Conditions $\mathrm{NST}_{\rm phys}$, $\mathrm{SS}$,
$\mathrm{ACTUAL\_SLOPE\_BRIDGE}$, $\mathrm{PAIR\_SAFE\_REFERENCE}$,
$\mathrm{PPE}_{N}$, $\mathrm{CEM\_DOM}$, and
$\mathrm{TENERGY}_{W}+\mathrm{CLOCK\_PROB}_{W}
+\mathrm{CLOCK\_SRC}_{W}$, together with the complete
positive stopped-source tree and its cemetery labels, give
Theorem~\ref{thm:direct-slope-small-ball}.  The high-frequency balanced
estimate is Proposition~\ref{prop:central-phase-verification}.  The regular
four-dimensional $M\times M$ defect is included in the low-frequency Proposition
\ref{prop:functional-correlation-low-frequency}.  Choosing
$K=e^{\eta_{\rm lo}\max\{m,n\}}$ and
$H=e^{\eta_{\rm hi}\max\{m,n\}}$ produces the low, intermediate-phase,
ultra-high observable, scale-bad, and regular-tail exponents in
\eqref{eq:final-CM2-exponent}.

Outside the diagonal band use Proposition
\ref{prop:signed-off-diagonal-mapping};
Lemma~\ref{lem:three-region} converts the three estimates to product decay.
For radial pieces Proposition~\ref{prop:radial-direct-Z}, Lemma
\ref{lem:FZ-angle-balance}, and Corollary
\ref{cor:radial-bi-recovery} give the common physical majorant without a
component-length estimate and with every component explicitly summed.
Proposition~\ref{prop:global-labelled-ledger} gives the global physical
ledger and its $L^{p_W}$ margin from the primitive hypotheses.  Lemma
\ref{lem:finite-depth-face-separation} and Proposition
\ref{prop:parameter-state-master-refinement} give the common coarea atlas at
each fixed depth.  Theorem~\ref{thm:face-two-cutoff} alone upgrades those
fixed cores through the middle band to $L(s)$.  Theorem
\ref{thm:shallow-two-threshold} removes shallow times beyond
$T_{\rm sh}(s)$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-s-collar-admissibility} and Theorem
\ref{thm:time-depth-diagonal} give the required $o(1)$ CM2-norm tail beyond
$L(s)$.  No fixed neighborhood is
claimed to be transverse at every depth, and no qualitative residual
assertion is used in this step.  The four fixed-term topologies,
$\mathrm{FACE}_{\rm 2CUT}$, and the fixed-term uniform ledgers meet in
\eqref{eq:typed-common-dominated-convergence}.  The shallow exponent window
converts its triangular bound into a common summable exponential array.
The deep block is different: Theorem~\ref{thm:time-depth-diagonal} gives
direct $\ell^1(\mathbb N^2)$-norm convergence to zero.  Thus the final
limit--double-sum exchange is dominated convergence for the fixed/shallow
blocks plus this separate uniform-integrability estimate for the deep
remainder; it is not claimed to be one literal application of
\eqref{eq:typed-common-dominated-convergence} to every triangular block.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Product-a.e. analytic paths and the circular boundary]
\label{cor:analytic-pathwise-CM2}
Assume all hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:global-cm2-assembly} other than
$\mathrm{QT}_{\rm JET}$, uniformly on the product-a.e. bases and directions
to which the analytic realization below applies.  In
particular this includes $\mathrm{QT}_{\rm LEDGER}$ and
\eqref{eq:constant-component-mass-vector}, as well as
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathrm{MT}_{\rm DQ},\ \mathrm{NST}_{\rm phys},\\
 \mathrm{ACTUAL\_SLOPE\_BRIDGE},\ \mathrm{PAIR\_SAFE\_REFERENCE},\\
 \mathrm{PPE}_{N},\ \mathrm{CEM\_DOM},\ \mathrm{TENERGY}_{W},\\
 \mathrm{DUAL\_MASS\_KERNEL},\ \mathrm{MPD},\
 \mathrm{POST\_CARRIER\_DOM},\ \mathrm{PHYS\_TEST\_LIFT},\\
 \mathrm{PHYS\_TREE\_MATCH},\ \mathrm{PROP\_Q\_MATCH},\\
 \mathrm{MAIN\_CLOCK\_COVERAGE},\
 \mathrm{SEL\_JSL}_{p_{\rm joint}},\\
 \mathrm{CLOCK\_PROB}_{W},\ \mathrm{CLOCK\_SRC}_{W},\
 \mathrm{KRH}_{p_W},\ \mathrm{USC}_{\rm end}^{W},\ \mathrm{BR}_{\rm sec},\\
 \mathrm{REC},\ \mathrm{FZ}_{\rm geom},\ \mathrm{SF}_{\rm split},\
 \mathrm{CP}_{\rm env},\ \mathrm{UCR}_{\rm sh},\\
 \mathrm{FACE}_{\rm 2CUT},\
 \mathrm{FACE\_TIME}_{\rm CM2}\text{ or }\mathrm{FACE\_TIME}_{\rm REC},\
 \mathrm{SHCOL}_{\rm env},\
 \mathrm{TAIL}_{\rm env}^{+},\ \mathrm{NREC}_{\rm deep}.
\end{gathered}
\]
If the analytic boundary family
satisfies the localized jet-direction condition
\eqref{eq:localized-analytic-jet-direction} and the exponent window
\eqref{eq:analytic-QT-window}, then for almost every base table in the
analytic product model, restricted to the gauge-compatible analytic chart,
and every admissible tangent direction with the
recorded derivative exponent, CM2 holds along the path.
The product randomness in this statement realizes only
$\mathrm{QT}_{\rm JET}$; it supplies neither
$\mathrm{QT}_{\rm LEDGER}$ nor any dynamical/source interface in the
preceding display.
For the finite-dimensional circular family, assume instead all hypotheses
of the theorem including the explicitly stated $\mathrm{QT}_{\rm path}$;
then the same conclusion holds only for center motions with fixed radii or
for constrained radius/shape paths satisfying
\eqref{eq:constant-component-mass-vector}.  No automatic circular
transversality or independent-radius gauge is asserted.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:analytic-QT-realization} to obtain
$\mathrm{QT}_{\rm JET}$.  Combine it with the separately assumed
$\mathrm{QT}_{\rm LEDGER}$, and then apply Theorem
\ref{thm:global-cm2-assembly}.  The last sentence is the constrained
circular branch of the latter theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Pathwise coarea-current convergence]
\label{prop:common-refinement-majorant}
In a compact table chart satisfying \textup{(H1)--(H5)}, assume
$\mathrm{QT}_{\rm path}$ and
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \mathrm{UCR}_{\rm sh},\ \mathrm{CEM\_DOM},\ \mathrm{TENERGY}_{W},\\
 \mathrm{ACTUAL\_SLOPE\_BRIDGE},\ \mathrm{PAIR\_SAFE\_REFERENCE},\
 \mathrm{PAIR\_TENERGY},\\
 \mathrm{DUAL\_MASS\_KERNEL},\ \mathrm{MPD},\
 \mathrm{POST\_CARRIER\_DOM},\ \mathrm{PHYS\_TEST\_LIFT},\\
 \mathrm{PHYS\_TREE\_MATCH},\ \mathrm{PROP\_Q\_MATCH},\\
 \mathrm{MAIN\_CLOCK\_COVERAGE},\
 \mathrm{SEL\_JSL}_{p_{\rm joint}},\\
 \mathrm{CLOCK\_PROB}_{W},\ \mathrm{CLOCK\_SRC}_{W},\
 \mathrm{KRH}_{p_W},\ \mathrm{FACE}_{\rm 2CUT},\\
 \text{one of }\mathrm{FACE\_TIME}_{\rm CM2},\
 \mathrm{FACE\_TIME}_{\rm REC},\\
 \mathrm{SHCOL}_{\rm env},\ \mathrm{TAIL}_{\rm env}^{+},\
 \mathrm{NREC}_{\rm deep}.
\end{gathered}
\]
Then all constants in the
functional-correlation, native-energy, fractional-phase, direct-$Z$, and
parameter-tail estimates are uniform up to $L(s)$.  The shallow coarea current
converges at every fixed depth in
\eqref{eq:face-DQ-weak-convergence} and satisfies
\eqref{eq:face-DQ-boundary-tightness}; the middle band is uniformly
integrable by \eqref{eq:face-middle-tail}, hence the growing-depth current
converges by Theorem~\ref{thm:face-two-cutoff}.  Independently, it has the
CM2 majorant \eqref{eq:face-time-CM2-majorant}, while the deep
remainder is $o(1)$ in the CM2 sum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The spacetime cells in $\mathrm{UCR}_{\rm sh}$, rather than compactness alone, make
hyperbolicity, distortion, the clean gate, moving branch sections, split
laws, connected cylinders, and single-face nondegeneracy uniform along the
whole coarea segment.  The
upper-scale excess tail and every energy-tree failure are uniform on the
same complete tilted labels by $\mathrm{USC}_{\rm end}^{W}$ and
$\mathrm{TENERGY}_{W}+\mathrm{CLOCK\_PROB}_{W}
+\mathrm{CLOCK\_SRC}_{W}$; no raw lattice cutoff or unweighted tail is used to
bound the complete marked phase mass.  The
fixed-depth coarea formula gives chartwise convergence of orientations and
amplitudes, hence bounded--Lipschitz convergence of the assembled current.
The uniform component-indexed boundary-$Z$ ledger bounds the mass of a
$\rho$-neighbourhood of the finite singularity set by
$C_{L,m,n}\rho^{\theta_{L,m,n}}$, which is exactly
\eqref{eq:face-DQ-boundary-tightness}.  Thus the current convergence may be
tested against the fixed-time branchwise observables; this is the same
weak-* plus boundary-neighbourhood mechanism as
\cite[Lemma~5.4]{CanestrariHoles}, now required on the common moving-face
atlas.  The per-depth positive coarea/return estimate
\eqref{eq:face-middle-per-depth} is then summed before any signed
recombination to obtain the middle tail with no $|s|^{-1}$ loss.  The
functional-correlation constants are uniform for a compact finite-horizon
family, and the return/context moments and both one-sided coupling estimates
are uniform by \textup{(H1)--(H2)}.  Proposition
\ref{prop:radial-direct-Z} fixes the component-indexed density/boundary
majorant.  Use the strict path margin
\eqref{eq:strict-jet-tail-margin}.  Proposition
\ref{prop:parameter-state-master-refinement} supplies the
depth-dependent scalar quotient tail on the same labels.  Apply the low/high
central estimates and off-diagonal couplings only through $T_{\rm sh}(s)$,
sum \eqref{eq:global-ledger-sum}, use Theorem
\ref{thm:shallow-two-threshold} for the remaining shallow times, and use Theorem
\ref{thm:time-depth-diagonal} for the deep part.
\end{proof}

The construction does not require a differentiable parameter family of
countable towers.  The coarea depth grows like $L(s)$, while the remaining
collision/grazing words are removed in the CM2 norm by the time--depth split
of Theorem~\ref{thm:time-depth-diagonal}, not by scalar total variation alone.

\section{A concrete relative-translation programme}

The abstract criterion becomes mathematically useful only after its hardest
interfaces are verified on one nonempty moving-table class.  This section
records the smallest class found in the present audit for which the known
billiard technology and the remaining new work can be separated cleanly.

\begin{definition}[Clean relative-translation class $\mathcal Q_{\rm tr}$]
\label{def:clean-relative-translation-class}
Let $O_1,\ldots,O_d$ be strictly convex analytic scatterers in the flat
two-torus, with $d\ge2$.  Fix $i_*$ and a nonzero vector $v$ and put
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:relative-translation-path}
 O_{i_*}(s)=O_{i_*}(0)+sv,\qquad O_i(s)=O_i(0)\quad(i\ne i_*).
\end{equation}
Assume that, for $|s|\le s_0$, the tables have no overlap, uniform finite
horizon, uniform strictly positive free flight, curvature in a fixed compact
interval, a common $C^3$ bound, and no triple singular intersection on the
finite clean gate used below.  All shapes and hence every component perimeter
are fixed.  Translation of only one scatterer is not a global conjugacy of
the billiard unless the remaining configuration has a special symmetry, so
this is a genuinely moving class.

Use arclength and outgoing angle on every component.  Then all collision
spaces are the same disjoint union $M$ and, exactly rather than up to a
Radon--Nikodym factor,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-common-SRB}
 d\widehat\mu_s=d\mu_0
 =\frac{\cos\varphi\,dr\,d\varphi}
        {2\sum_i|\partial O_i|}.
\end{equation}
Thus the component-mass obstruction
\eqref{eq:constant-component-mass-vector} is absent and the
measure-trivializing gauge may be taken to be the common arclength gauge.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Stationary and sequential literature package]
\label{prop:translation-literature-package}
On a sufficiently small compact subchart of $\mathcal Q_{\rm tr}$, the
uniform hyperbolicity, growth, distortion, and proper-family recovery
constants can be chosen independently of the table.  For each fixed $s$,
stationary repetition of $F_s$ preserves the common collision measure
\eqref{eq:translation-common-SRB}; once a coarea component has independently
been shown to be a proper regular standard family, stationary coupling gives
the forward centered estimate in
\eqref{eq:face-time-recovered-correlation}, and the reverse estimate follows
after applying the billiard time-reversal involution to the separately typed
reverse representation.  For an admissible nonautonomous small-step
sequence, the literature instead gives uniform two-law loss of memory.  It
does not assert that the cross-configuration maps preserve $\mu_0$ or
directly give a fixed-$\mu_0$ centered sequential correlation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The common phase space and the invariant probability for each stationary
$F_s$ are \eqref{eq:translation-common-SRB}.  Uniform perturbation and
spectral stability for movements and deformations of Lorentz scatterers are
proved in \cite{DemersZhangPerturb}; uniform exponential two-law loss of
memory for arbitrary small-step moving-scatterer sequences is proved in
\cite{SYZ}.  The latter comparison is not recast as invariance of the usual
collision measure across different configurations.  The positive
standard-family mechanism needed after restriction is consistent with the
long-curve/small-bad-part hypothesis of \cite{CanestrariPerturb} and with the
conditional invariance and exponential mixing of standard families in
\cite{CanestrariHoles}.  Those papers do \emph{not} prove that every moving
coarea face in the present problem is such a family, nor do they prove the
actual-SRB polynomial escape or the occurrencewise likelihood below.  These
remain explicit gates rather than being hidden inside this proposition.
\end{proof}

The first new gate replaces the full prescribed-depth escape statement by a
concrete derivative test.  Write $D=d_Z+1$.

\begin{definition}[Relative-translation prescribed-depth jet gate
$\mathrm{TR\_PPE}_{\rm JET}$]
\label{def:translation-ppe-jet}
On each stopped conditional $Q$, use a normalized native coordinate
$z\in[-1,1]$.  At depth $N$ there is an exceptional subset of conditional
mass at most $Ce^{-b_{\rm tr}N}$.  Its complement is the union of at most
$Ce^{a_{\rm cmp}N}$ intervals.  On each interval require a clean
finite-holonomy truncation at $L=\lceil\kappa_hN\rceil$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-finite-holonomy-truncation}
 X^{[N]}_{\beta,Q}=X^{[N,L]}_{\beta,Q}+\mathcal R_{N,L},
 \qquad \|\mathcal R_{N,L}\|_\infty\le Ce^{-c_hL}\le r/100.
\end{equation}
The finite-prefix function $X^{[N,L]}_{\beta,Q}$ is analytic on the clean
analytic-flight chart and satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-ppe-derivative-gate}
 \inf_z\left|\partial_z^{D}X^{[N,L]}_{\beta,Q}(z)\right|
 \ge e^{-a_{\rm jet}N}.
\end{equation}
The normalized collision--SRB density in this coordinate is bounded by a
uniform constant.  The same constants work on every frozen context used by
$\mathrm{SS}(d_Z)$.  Require
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-ppe-positive-window}
 C_{\rm buf}(a_{\rm jet}+Da_{\rm cmp})<1.
\end{equation}
The truncation window also requires $c_h\kappa_h>C_{\rm buf}^{-1}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Jet gate supplies the PPE small-ball clauses]
\label{lem:translation-jet-implies-ppe}
Assume Definition~\ref{def:translation-ppe-jet}.  Then clause
\textup{(PPE1)} holds with every exponent
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-ppe-exponent}
 0<\Theta_{\rm PPE}<
 \min\left\{b_{\rm tr}C_{\rm buf},
 \frac{1-C_{\rm buf}(a_{\rm jet}+Da_{\rm cmp})}{D}\right\}.
\end{equation}
The weighted-amplitude estimate
\eqref{eq:ppe-weighted-amplitude-sublevel} and the exact mark/moment
requirements \eqref{eq:exact-local-ppe-mark}--
\eqref{eq:ppe-mark-conditional-moment} of \textup{(PPE2)} remain
additional.  If those conditions, the retained-continuation and resolving-buffer clause
\textup{(PPE3)} from $\mathrm{NST}_{\rm phys}$, and the finite-$s$
$\mathrm{UCR}_{\rm sh}$ uniformity clause \textup{(PPE4)} are verified on
the same descendants, then the full $\mathrm{PPE}_{N}(d_Z)$ interface
holds.  None of these three additions follows from the derivative gate.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For $P\in\mathbb R_{d_Z}[z]$ the $D$th derivative of
$X^{[N,L]}_{\beta,Q}-P$ is the left side of
\eqref{eq:translation-ppe-derivative-gate}.  The one-dimensional
finite-type sublevel estimate on each good interval gives
\[
 |\{|X^{[N,L]}_{\beta,Q}-P|\le Cr\}|
 \le C(r e^{a_{\rm jet}N})^{1/D}.
\]
Sum over at most $Ce^{a_{\rm cmp}N}$ intervals and add the exceptional
mass.  With $N=\lceil C_{\rm buf}\log(1/r)\rceil$, the two terms have the
powers displayed in \eqref{eq:translation-ppe-exponent}.  This proof uses
the actual absolutely continuous conditional and never renormalizes a thin
surviving Cantor set.  The last inequality in
\eqref{eq:translation-finite-holonomy-truncation} transfers the same bound,
after changing the fixed constant, to $X^{[N]}_{\beta,Q}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Terminal rough-gate alternative
$\mathrm{TR\_PPE}_{\rm TERM}$]
\label{def:translation-ppe-terminal}
Put $D=d_Z+1$ and
$N=\lceil C_{\rm buf}\log(1/r)\rceil$.  Suppose the native conditional
$Q$, of diameter comparable to
$\sigma(r)=r^{1/(1+\alpha_L)}$, has a native recovery coefficient
$C_g>0$ and enters a uniform regular-family class after
at most
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-terminal-recovery-fraction}
 R_Q\le \rho_{\rm rec}N+C,\qquad
 \rho_{\rm rec}:=\frac{C_g}{(1+\alpha_L)C_{\rm buf}},
\end{equation}
outside mass $Ce^{-b_{\rm rec}N}$.  Fix
$0<\theta_0<\theta_1<1-\rho_{\rm rec}$ and one of the following two gate
mechanisms, before the linked query.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(G\arabic*)}]
\item There are a finite rough gate $\mathcal G$, a block length $n_0$,
      and $p_0>0$ such that every normalized family in the regular class
      hits $\mathcal G$ in the next $n_0$-block with conditional probability
      at least $p_0$.  Restriction to a miss and renormalization stays in the
      same class.  Put
      \begin{equation}
      \label{eq:translation-terminal-gate-rate}
       b_{\rm gate}:=\frac{|\log(1-p_0)|}{n_0}.
      \end{equation}
\item There is a uniform, possibly time-dependent, rough-gate family
      $\mathcal G_j$ and a record-preserving stopped antichain of its first
      entrance pieces such that, from every recovered proper family, the
      mass not yet assigned to an entrance piece after $t$ collisions is at
      most $Ce^{-b_{\rm gate}t}$.  No assertion that each normalized
      remainder is immediately regular is made in this renewal version.
\end{enumerate}
Ordinary mixing by itself supplies neither mechanism.  At
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-terminal-restart-time}
 j_-:=\lceil(1-\theta_1)N\rceil
\end{equation}
restart the chosen gate/renewal mechanism on the then-proper image family;
properness at this restart is part of the terminal input.  Ignore earlier
gate visits and take the first accepted entrance after $j_-$, requiring
$j\le\lfloor(1-\theta_0)N\rfloor$.  Thus its remaining length satisfies
$k=N-j\in[\theta_0N+O(1),\theta_1N+O(1)]$.

For each admissible first-hit time $j$, the union of all first-hit entrance
cells, after removal of original conditional mass
$Ce^{-b_{\rm rough}k}$, continues to a disjoint union of at most
$Ce^{a_{\rm cmp}k}$ clean intervals in the original native $z$ coordinate.
Uniformly on those intervals, in the
frozen context and
$P\in\mathbb R_{d_Z}[z]$, require
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-terminal-jet}
 \inf_z|\partial_z^D X^{[N,L]}_{\beta,Q}(z)|
 \ge e^{-a_{\rm jet}k},\qquad
 L=\lceil\kappa_hN\rceil,\qquad
 \|\mathcal R_{N,L}\|_\infty\le Ce^{-c_hL}\le r/100.
\end{equation}
The collision--SRB conditional density in this same coordinate is uniformly
bounded.  All entrance cells, misses, and continuation records are Borel
restrictions of the same stopped descendant law used by
$\mathrm{PPE}_N(d_Z)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Invariant extended-jet cone criterion]
\label{prop:translation-terminal-jet-cone}
Let $D=d_Z+1$.  Suppose that on every retained terminal branch the truncated
endpoint phase has the exact affine recurrence
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-affine-jet-recurrence}
 X_{t+1}(z)=a_{b,t}(z)+b_{b,t}(z)
                 X_t(\phi_{b,t}(z)).
\end{equation}
For the extended jet
$\widetilde J_DX=(1,X,X',\ldots,X^{(D)})$, let
$\mathcal A_{b,t}(z)$ be the exact Fa\`a di Bruno matrix, so that
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-extended-jet-cocycle}
 \widetilde J_DX_{t+1}(z)
 =\mathcal A_{b,t}(z)
  \widetilde J_DX_t(\phi_{b,t}z).
\end{equation}
Assume there is a closed orientation-recorded cone field $\mathscr C_b(z)$
such that, uniformly over every depth, frozen context, and retained branch,
\begin{align}
 \mathcal A_{b,t}(z)\mathscr C_{b'}(\phi_{b,t}z)
 &\subset\mathscr C_b(z),
 \label{eq:translation-jet-cone-invariance}\\
 |(\mathcal A_{b,t}(z)v)_D|
 &\ge e^{-a_0}|v_D|,
 \qquad v\in\mathscr C_{b'}(\phi_{b,t}z).
 \label{eq:translation-jet-cone-expansion}
\end{align}
If every accepted terminal seed lies in the cone and satisfies
$|X_{\rm seed}^{(D)}|\ge c_0>0$, then after $k$ terminal steps
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-jet-cone-conclusion}
 |\partial_z^DX^{[N,L]}(z)|\ge c_0e^{-a_0k}.
\end{equation}
Consequently, for any $a_{\rm jet}>a_0$,
\eqref{eq:translation-terminal-jet} holds whenever
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-jet-seed-threshold}
 k\ge k_0:=\max\left\{0,
 \left\lceil\frac{\log(c_0^{-1})}{a_{\rm jet}-a_0}\right\rceil\right\},
\end{equation}
provided its separate analytic-remainder, component-count, and bad-mass
clauses hold.  Since the terminal construction has
$k\ge\theta_0N+O(1)$, its small-$r$ window may be reduced so that
\eqref{eq:translation-jet-seed-threshold} holds.  At the remaining finitely
many depths one keeps the factor $c_0$ in the finite-type constant; no
unit-prefactor jet bound is asserted there without a separate check.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first coordinate in $\widetilde J_D$ makes the derivatives of
$a_{b,t}$ part of a linear extended-jet map.  Apply
\eqref{eq:translation-jet-cone-invariance} inductively and multiply the
lower bounds in \eqref{eq:translation-jet-cone-expansion}.  This gives
\eqref{eq:translation-jet-cone-conclusion}.  If
$k\ge k_0$, then $c_0e^{-a_0k}\ge e^{-a_{\rm jet}k}$, which is exactly
\eqref{eq:translation-terminal-jet}.  The terminal lower bound on $k$ lets
one enforce this by shrinking the declared small-$r$ window.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weighted good-cell PPE bound without a second component loss]
\label{lem:translation-weighted-good-cell-PPE}
On the accepted terminal components suppose
\eqref{eq:translation-terminal-jet} holds for a remaining length
$k\in[\theta_0N+O(1),\theta_1N+O(1)]$.  Let $A$, $B_{C,y}$,
$Z_A$, and $\mathfrak m_{\rm PPE}$ be exactly those of
\textup{(PPE2)}.  Then, uniformly in
$P\in\mathbb R_{d_Z}[z]$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-weighted-good-cell-bound}
 \mu^{u,A}\{|X^{[N,L]}-P|\le r;\ {\rm good}\}
 \le C\mathfrak m_{\rm PPE}
       (re^{a_{\rm jet}k})^{1/D}.
\end{equation}
There is no additional factor $e^{a_{\rm cmp}k}$: the sum of the actual
component densities is already part of
\eqref{eq:exact-local-ppe-mark}.

If in addition the tilted bad mass satisfies, on the same stopped parent,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-weighted-bad-mass}
 Z_A^{-1}\int_{\rm bad}A\,d\mu^u
 \le C\mathfrak m_{\rm PPE}e^{-b_Wk},
\end{equation}
then \eqref{eq:ppe-weighted-amplitude-sublevel} holds with every exponent
smaller than
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-weighted-ppe-cone-exponent}
 \min\left\{C_{\rm buf}\theta_0b_W,
 \frac{1-C_{\rm buf}\theta_1a_{\rm jet}}D\right\},
 \qquad C_{\rm buf}\theta_1a_{\rm jet}<1.
\end{equation}
The complete PPE exponent is the minimum with the raw
\textup{(PPE1)} exponent, which still contains the component-count loss.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On one good component, the finite-type sublevel estimate and the density
$B_{C,y}$ give
\[
 C Z_A^{-1}\|B_{C,y}\|_\infty
 (re^{a_{\rm jet}k})^{1/D}.
\]
Sum over $C$ and use
\eqref{eq:exact-local-ppe-mark}; this proves
\eqref{eq:translation-weighted-good-cell-bound}.  Add
\eqref{eq:translation-weighted-bad-mass}, substitute
$N=\lceil C_{\rm buf}\log(1/r)\rceil$, and use the two endpoint bounds on
$k$.  The resulting powers are exactly
\eqref{eq:translation-weighted-ppe-cone-exponent}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A noncircular conditional-moment route for PPE2]
\label{prop:translation-PPE-Lyapunov-route}
Fix one tuple
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-PPE-uniform-Lyapunov-data}
 (V_0,\rho,C,b_W,p_{\rm PPE}),
 \qquad V_0,C,b_W<\infty,\quad 0<\rho<1,
 \quad p_{\rm PPE}>1,
\end{equation}
independent of $s,r,N,j$, the registry amplitude, the frozen context, and
the choice of global, shallow, or stopped-deep parent.  Let
$(\mathcal V_t)_{0\le t\le N}$ be one nonnegative, adapted, integrable Borel
Lyapunov field on each corresponding exact stopped descendant kernel,
restarted with the same data \eqref{eq:translation-PPE-uniform-Lyapunov-data}
on every such parent law.
Disintegrate first by the immutable first-hit record $j$, so $k=N-j$ is
fixed on the resulting cell, and continue every accepted, missed, and
cemetery record to the deterministic prescribed depth $N=N(r)$.  Write
$b_N:=Z_A^{-1}\int_{\rm bad}A\,d\mu^u$ on that cell.  Assume, on each such
parent and record cell,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-PPE-Lyapunov-domination}
 \mathfrak m_{\rm PPE}^{p_{\rm PPE}}\le\mathcal V_N,
 \qquad
 b_N\le e^{-b_Wk}
 \left(\mathbb E[\mathcal V_N\mid\mathcal F_{\rm parent}]
 \right)^{1/p_{\rm PPE}}.
\end{equation}
The second inequality is a structural tilted-bad-set domination by the same
Lyapunov field, not the desired PPE sublevel estimate.  Suppose at every
restart $\mathcal V_0\le V_0$ almost surely and, on the same conditional
kernel up to the deterministic continuation depth $N$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-PPE-Lyapunov-drift}
 \mathbb E[\mathcal V_{t+1}\mid\mathcal F_t]
 \le\rho\mathcal V_t+C.
\end{equation}
Then \eqref{eq:ppe-mark-conditional-moment} and
\eqref{eq:translation-weighted-bad-mass} hold uniformly on all three
restarted laws.  Together with
Lemma~\ref{lem:translation-weighted-good-cell-PPE}, this verifies the
weighted and mark-moment part of \textup{(PPE2)} without assuming its
conclusion in the input.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Iterating \eqref{eq:translation-PPE-Lyapunov-drift} to the prescribed time
$N$ gives, on every parent and immutable $j$-record cell,
\[
 \mathbb E[\mathcal V_N\mid\mathcal F_{\rm parent}]
 \le V_0+\frac C{1-\rho}.
\]
Apply the two dominations in
\eqref{eq:translation-PPE-Lyapunov-domination}.  The first gives the required
conditional $p_{\rm PPE}$ moment.  The second gives
$b_N\le C e^{-b_Wk}\le C\mathfrak m_{\rm PPE}e^{-b_Wk}$ because the exact
mark in \eqref{eq:exact-local-ppe-mark} is at least one.  This is
\eqref{eq:translation-weighted-bad-mass}.  If $Z_{A,j}$ is the amplitude
normalizer of the $j$-cell, then recombination uses the genuine tilted
mixture weights $w_j:=Z_{A,j}/Z_A$, with $\sum_jw_j=1$.  The cellwise bad
ratios are averaged linearly with these weights.  The recombined mark is
$\mathfrak m_{\rm PPE}=\sum_jw_j\mathfrak m_{{\rm PPE},j}$, so conditional
Jensen gives the explicit countable-recombination estimate
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-PPE-recombination-Jensen}
 \mathfrak m_{\rm PPE}^{p_{\rm PPE}}
 \le\sum_jw_j\mathfrak m_{{\rm PPE},j}^{p_{\rm PPE}},
 \qquad
 \mathbb E[\mathfrak m_{\rm PPE}^{p_{\rm PPE}}
       \mid\mathcal F_{\rm parent}]
 \le V_0+\frac C{1-\rho}.
\end{equation}
Thus the same bound survives the countable recombination without an
unrecorded component-count loss.  No optional-stopping estimate for a
randomly selected terminal value $\mathcal V_T$ is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite-operation exhaustive amplitude registry]
\label{lem:translation-PPE-finite-word-registry}
Let $\mathfrak J_0$ be a standard-Borel set of initial amplitude generators
and let $\mathscr O$ be a standard-Borel alphabet containing every allowed
branch restriction, Jacobian multiplication, holonomy pushforward, test
lift, owner handler, and cemetery continuation.  Assume every operation and
the evaluation of every finite operation word are jointly Borel on their
declared carrier domains.  Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-PPE-word-registry}
 \mathfrak J_{\rm PPE}
 :=\bigsqcup_{k\ge0}\mathfrak J_0\times\mathscr O^k
\end{equation}
is standard Borel.  If every amplitude actually used by the proof is the
value of one finite operation word uniquely fixed by the immutable
occurrence record before $P$ is chosen, and the record-to-word map into
the whole disjoint union \eqref{eq:translation-PPE-word-registry} is Borel
(equivalently, the length map and each restriction to its Borel length
stratum are Borel), this registry is query-independent and exhaustive in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:physical-legal-extension}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Finite products and countable disjoint unions of standard-Borel spaces are
standard Borel.  Composing the stipulated Borel record-to-word map with the
jointly Borel evaluation gives the required amplitude field.  Exhaustivity
is exactly the hypothesis that every used amplitude has such a word.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[An $s=0$ all-depth jet does not imply PPE4]
\label{prop:translation-PPE4-parameter-countermodel}
Let $X_s^{[N]}(z)=(1-se^{aN})z^D$.  At $s=0$ its $D$th derivative is nonzero
at every depth, but for $N(s)\sim a^{-1}\log(1/|s|)$ it vanishes.  Therefore
\textup{(PPE4)} requires parameter-uniform cone, drift, registry, and moving
cut bounds up to $N\le L(s)$; an $s=0$ all-depth cone is insufficient.  A
simple sufficient scale separation that preserves a uniform fraction of the
$s=0$ top-jet bound is
$|s|e^{aL(s)}\to0$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The $D$th derivative equals $D!(1-se^{aN})$.  Take depths for which
$se^{aN}=1$ (or tends to one along integer depths).  This cancels the top
jet although the entire $s=0$ sequence is uniformly nonzero.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Terminal gate supplies the PPE small-ball clause]
\label{lem:translation-terminal-implies-ppe}
Under $\mathrm{TR\_PPE}_{\rm TERM}$, clause \textup{(PPE1)} holds with
every
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-terminal-ppe-exponent}
\begin{split}
0<\Theta_{\rm PPE}<\min\bigg\{&C_{\rm buf}b_{\rm rec},
 C_{\rm buf}b_{\rm gate}(\theta_1-\theta_0),
 C_{\rm buf}b_{\rm rough}\theta_0,\\
 &\frac{1-C_{\rm buf}\theta_1
 (a_{\rm jet}+Da_{\rm cmp})}{D}\bigg\},
\end{split}
\end{equation}
provided
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-terminal-positive-window}
 \rho_{\rm rec}+\theta_1<1,\qquad
 C_{\rm buf}\theta_1(a_{\rm jet}+Da_{\rm cmp})<1,
 \qquad c_h\kappa_hC_{\rm buf}>1.
\end{equation}
The weighted-amplitude and exact mark/moment clause \textup{(PPE2)}, the
continuation/buffer clause \textup{(PPE3)}, and the finite-$s$ clause
\textup{(PPE4)} remain additional on exactly the same entrance cells.  If
continuation has another bad exponent, it is inserted as one more
term $C_{\rm buf}b_{\rm cont}\theta_0$ in the minimum.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Under \textup{(G1)} there are at least
$\lfloor(\theta_1-\theta_0)N/n_0\rfloor$ admissible attempts, and iteration
of the block hazard gives missed-gate mass
$Ce^{-b_{\rm gate}(\theta_1-\theta_0)N}$.  Under \textup{(G2)} this is the
stated stopped-antichain tail over the same window.  Sum the finite-type sublevel
bound over the $O(N)$ possible first-hit blocks and at most
$Ce^{a_{\rm cmp}\theta_1N}$ clean cells.  The good mass is at most
\[
 CN e^{a_{\rm cmp}\theta_1N}
       (r e^{a_{\rm jet}\theta_1N})^{1/D}.
\]
The strict last exponent in
\eqref{eq:translation-terminal-ppe-exponent} absorbs the factor $N$.
The recovery and missed-gate terms give the first two exponents.  Summing the
rough-continuation mass over the $O(N)$ hit times gives the third; its strict
inequality absorbs this polynomial factor as well.  The last inequality in
\eqref{eq:translation-terminal-positive-window} transfers the truncated
phase estimate to the full phase.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the terminal gate still requires]
\label{rem:translation-terminal-rough-transfer}
Propositions~4.31--4.33 of \cite{Leclerc2026} supply an all-polynomial,
all-scale roughness mechanism on the unstable Cantor conditional of an
Axiom--A basic set.  They do not supply
\eqref{eq:translation-terminal-jet} for the absolutely continuous
collision--SRB conditional.  A valid billiard application must separately
prove that the gate has uniform positive hazard for the actual regular
families together with miss-conditioned stability or an equivalent stopped
renewal tail, and that the
gate-to-native coordinate transfer preserves degree-$d_Z$ polynomials
modulo the charged remainder.  Thus the terminal construction reduces the
length over which losses accumulate; it does not replace the missing
rough-transfer theorem.
The time-dependent coupling magnet of \cite[Sections~5--7 and
Corollary~34]{SYZ} supplies a model for \textup{(G2)} after proper recovery:
its uncoupled remainder has a uniform geometric renewal tail even though
individual leftovers need not remain proper.  In its block notation one may
take
$b_{\rm gate}=\Delta^{-1}|\log(1-\widetilde\zeta/2)|$.
Corollary~\ref{cor:fixed-section-pilot-finite-type-gate} now puts a
parameter-uniform degree-one finite-type seed on one actual clean
unstable interval of the pilot.  What neither result supplies is the exact
identification of the coupling entrance coordinate with the endpoint-ratio
phase in \eqref{eq:actual-derivative-ratio-slope}, followed by a
record-preserving continuation estimate that keeps the seed on the extracted
magnet reference pieces.  This seed-to-magnet identification, rather than
the existence of a local nonlinear orbit or an exponential entrance hazard,
is the remaining new terminal-PPE theorem.
\end{remark}

The second gate makes the change of law and the physical/source
identification one calculation rather than two unrelated assumptions.

\begin{definition}[Relative-translation matching gate
$\mathrm{TR\_MATCH}$]
\label{def:translation-match}
For every preselection parent $\eta$, let the source occurrence envelope
$q_\eta$ be the restriction of the datum-independent master kernel and set
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-proposal}
 \zeta_\eta(da)=\frac{q_\eta(da)}{q_\eta(Y_{\rm src})}
\end{equation}
when the denominator is nonzero.  On
$\{q_\eta(Y_{\rm src})=0\}$ set $\zeta_\eta:=\zeta_0$ for one fixed
$\zeta_0\in\mathcal P(Y_{\rm src})$.  Thus
$\zeta_\eta(Y_{\rm src})=1$ for every parent, including a zero-source
parent; this harmless convention is Borel and is fixed before every main
selector.  On each clean coarea component require a record-preserving Borel
holonomy/translation map $H_{\eta,a}$ from the probability carrier to the
physical source carrier such that
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-match-bounds}
 \frac{d\Pi^{\eta}}{dK^{\rm prob}}\le Ce^{a_{\rm tree}N},\qquad
 q_\eta(Y_{\rm src})\le Ce^{a_qN},\qquad
 \left\|\frac{d\nu_{\eta,a}}
 {d(H_{\eta,a*}\kappa_\eta)}\right\|_\infty
 \le Ce^{a_cN}.
\end{equation}
Here $\Pi^{\eta}$ is the exact $\eta$-marginal of the complete-tree law
used in the numerator of the source occurrence measure, so the displayed
Radon--Nikodym derivative is between measures on the same space.
The map preserves the full immutable occurrence record and the exact
coarea $m$-current, and its actual slope obeys the event preimage inclusion
of $\mathrm{CARRIER\_MATCH}$ at the exact active window for every
$\kappa\in\{r,\mathrm{PPE},N_{\rm time}\}$; an $r$-clock row is not inferred
from the PPE inclusion.  Moreover the physical coefficient and propagated
occurrence measures admit one record-preserving Markov kernel
$\mathcal K^{\rm occ}$, fixed before the linked query, for which
\eqref{eq:prop-q-exact-m-match},
\eqref{eq:propagated-q-physical-match}, and the exact scalar-current
identity following those displays hold on every record-measurable
restriction.  Thus the source $q$ is the dominated pushforward of the
physical occurrence envelope, not an independently enlarged finite
measure.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Concrete selection-safe likelihood]
\label{lem:translation-match-implies-jsl}
Under $\mathrm{TR\_MATCH}$ the physical/source bridge is exact.  Moreover,
$\mathrm{SEL\_JSL}_\infty$ holds with
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-likelihood-loss}
 a_L\le a_{\rm tree}+a_q+2a_c.
\end{equation}
The same statement with $L^{p_o}$ and $L^{p_c}$ bounds gives the joint
exponent of Lemma~\ref{lem:joint-source-factor-criterion}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use \eqref{eq:translation-proposal} in the selection-safe lifted reference
law.  The occurrence factor contains the selector, which is at most one,
the tree density, and $q_\eta(Y_{\rm src})$; the two carrier coordinates
each contribute the last density in
\eqref{eq:translation-match-bounds}.  Up to the uniformly bounded
$M_*^{-1}$ normalization, their product is the density $Z_A$, giving
\eqref{eq:translation-likelihood-loss}.  The support,
event-inclusion, exact-current, and record statements are part of
$\mathrm{TR\_MATCH}$, so no selected conditional law or parallel physical
ledger is introduced.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Diagonal common-law realization of
$\mathrm{TR\_MATCH}$]
\label{prop:translation-diagonal-match}
Suppose the complete physical tree and the source construction are made on
one unselected parent law, and, parent by parent,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-diagonal-match-data}
 \Pi^\eta=K^{\rm prob},\qquad
 \nu_{\eta,a}=H_{\eta,a*}\kappa_\eta,
 \qquad
 (m,q)_{\rm src}=\mathcal K^{\rm occ}_*(m,q)_{\rm phys}
\end{equation}
as signed current and dominating positive envelope on every
record-measurable restriction.  Assume that the identity occurrence kernel
is record preserving, that the exact active-slope event pulls back under
$H_{\eta,a}$, and that
$q_\eta(Y_{\rm src})\le Ce^{a_qN}$.  Then
$\mathrm{TR\_MATCH}$ holds with $a_{\rm tree}=a_c=0$, and its unique lifted
likelihood is, up to the uniformly bounded normalization,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-diagonal-match-likelihood}
 Z_A=M_*^{-1}\one_A q_\eta(Y_{\rm src}),
 \qquad a_L\le a_q.
\end{equation}
On a zero-source parent the selected source restriction is zero and the
fixed convention $\zeta_0$ above changes neither current nor likelihood.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
All three nontrivial Radon--Nikodym ratios in
\eqref{eq:translation-match-bounds} are one.  The proposal cancels the
conditional $q_\eta$ factor and leaves only its total mass.  The exact
signed-current and positive-envelope identities in
\eqref{eq:translation-diagonal-match-data} are precisely
$\mathrm{PROP\_Q\_MATCH}$, rather than a comparison of independently
chosen source and physical ledgers.  The event inclusion and immutable
record statements complete $\mathrm{CARRIER\_MATCH}$ and give
\eqref{eq:translation-diagonal-match-likelihood}.
\end{proof}

The third gate joins face time to the shallow/deep mechanism instead of
postulating decay of the full positive face mass.

\begin{definition}[Relative-translation coarea recovery gate
$\mathrm{TR\_FACE\_REC}$]
\label{def:translation-face-recovery}
The moving coarea current, after the constant-polarity decomposition, has
the immutable side-specific recovery payload $\mathscr P_{\rm rec}$ and a
fixed number $0<\delta<1$.  Its constants obey
\[
 c_R,c_{\rm or},c_{\rm mix},c_{\rm ret},A_{\rm face},
 c_{\rm tail},A_{\rm loss}>0,\qquad
 \kappa_{\rm rec},a_{\rm fw}\ge0.
\]
At the linked query define the complete deep restriction
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-complete-deep-owner}
 I_{\rm deep,>L}(s,m,n):=
 \bigl(I_{\rm deep}(\varrho)\sqcup
       I_{\rm main}^{\rm deep}(\vartheta)\bigr)
       \cap\{a:\ell_{\rm word}(a)>L\}.
\end{equation}
It contains both main and nonmain word owners.  Require
\begin{align}
 \sup_{\mathfrak o\in\{-,+\}}
 \overline M^q\{a:\mathfrak o\in\operatorname{Acc}(a),\
                  R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}(a)>u\}
 &\le Ce^{-c_Ru},
 \label{eq:translation-recovery-tail}\\
 \overline M^q(I_{\rm fw}(s,m,n))
 &\le Ce^{a_{\rm fw}\max\{m,n\}},
 \label{eq:translation-face-envelope-growth}\\
 \overline M^q\{\ell_{\rm word}=\ell\}
 &\le C_{m,n}e^{-(c_{\rm ret}-A_{\rm face})\ell},
 \label{eq:translation-word-tail}\\
 \overline M^q(I_{\rm deep,>L})
 &\le Ce^{-(c_{\rm tail}-A_{\rm loss})L}.
 \label{eq:translation-deep-tail}
\end{align}
For $N=\max\{m,n\}$ put
\[
 \operatorname{Long}(m,n):=\{\mathfrak o\in\{-,+\}:
                     d_{\mathfrak o}(m,n)=N\}
\]
and define the active-side mismatch filter
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-orientation-mismatch}
 I_{\rm mis}(s,m,n):=
 \{a\in I_{\rm fw}(s,m,n):
       \operatorname{Acc}(a)\cap\operatorname{Long}(m,n)=\varnothing\}.
\end{equation}
It is required that
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-orientation-mismatch-tail}
 \overline M^q(I_{\rm mis}(s,m,n))\le Ce^{-c_{\rm or}N}.
\end{equation}
On $I_{\rm fw}(s,m,n)\setminus I_{\rm mis}$ choose measurably
$\mathfrak o_*(a;m,n)$ to minimize
$R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}(a)$ over
$\operatorname{Acc}(a)\cap\operatorname{Long}(m,n)$, with a fixed tie
order, and put
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-active-recovery-tail}
 I_{R,\rm tail}(s,m,n):=
 \{a\in I_{\rm fw}(s,m,n)\setminus I_{\rm mis}:
   R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o_*(a;m,n)}(a)>\delta N\}.
\end{equation}
The two-sided bound \eqref{eq:translation-recovery-tail} gives
$\overline M^q(I_{R,\rm tail})\le 2Ce^{-\delta c_RN}$.  Every recovered
cell below uses this selected tuple and its actual
$R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o_*}$.
The first image of every recovered component is a regular standard family
in the uniform class of Proposition~\ref{prop:translation-literature-package};
the forward or reverse long-side test is centered and obeys its coupling
estimate with exponent $c_{\rm mix}$.  More precisely, outside
$I_{\rm mis}$ every recovered face component uses a pre-recorded tuple on a
side in $\operatorname{Acc}(a)\cap\operatorname{Long}(m,n)$ and, at
the recorded integer time $R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}$, its tangent lies in the
corresponding unstable cone and its normalized density is regular, with
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-recorded-recovery-clock}
 R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}
 \le \kappa_{\rm rec}\ell_{\rm word}+C.
\end{equation}
The
two alternative tuples, when present, both obey
\eqref{eq:alternative-recovery-exact-current} and share one coefficient;
no new side, source decomposition, or positive mass is created by the
query.  A component for which neither
orientation is available must belong to $I_{\rm deep,>L}$ and is controlled by
\eqref{eq:translation-deep-tail}; it may not remain in the shallow
face/word core.  This
$\mathrm{ORIENTED\_FACE\_ACCESS}$ clause is not inferred from analyticity:
a preimage-of-grazing face can run directly into the phase boundary.  The
word estimate is the positive coarea estimate before recombination and
contains no $|s|^{-1}$.  More precisely, for both the finite-$s$ current
and its $s=0$ limit, the exact derivative-weighted coarea density
$\mathcal M_{\lambda,m,n}$ is the record-preserving pushforward of the same
physical/source occurrence restriction supplied by $\mathrm{TR\_MATCH}$,
and it satisfies the full per-depth estimate
\eqref{eq:face-middle-per-depth} with the same
$c_{\rm ret},A_{\rm face}$ and $C_{m,n}$.  Thus
\eqref{eq:translation-word-tail} is not an unweighted count of labels and
does not replace either positive-current clause of
$\mathrm{FACE}_{\rm 2CUT}$.  The
shallow collar is required, on every linked shallow cell and with the same
restricted $q$, to satisfy all three clauses
$\mathrm{SHSRC}^{+}$, $\mathrm{SHCP}_{\rm env}$, and
$\mathrm{SHREC}(q_{\rm sh})$ of
Definition~\ref{def:shallow-collar-envelope}, through either
\eqref{eq:normalized-shallow-recovery} or
\eqref{eq:aggregate-shallow-recovery}.  The deep remainder similarly
satisfies $\mathrm{TAIL}_{\rm env}^{+}$ and
$\mathrm{NREC}_{\rm deep}$ through either
\eqref{eq:weighted-deep-recovery} or
\eqref{eq:aggregate-deep-recovery}; a bare scalar tail without a
recovery-weighted aggregate is insufficient.  Finally the strict windows
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-recovery-windows}
 c_{\rm ret}>A_{\rm face},\qquad
 \kappa_L(c_{\rm tail}-A_{\rm loss})>1,
 \qquad (1-\delta)c_{\rm mix}>a_{\rm fw},
 \qquad \eqref{eq:shallow-time-window}
\end{equation}
hold.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Normalized boundary-complexity recovery subgate
$\mathrm{TR\_Z\_REC}$]
\label{def:translation-Z-recovery}
For every accessible orientation $\mathfrak o$, push the corresponding
positive coarea component for a uniformly bounded geometric entry time to a
measured unstable family $\mathcal G_a^{\mathfrak o}$.  This entry map,
orientation, and family are Borel, record preserving, and fixed before the
linked query.  Let $H_a^{\mathfrak o}$ be its scaled log--H\"older density
mark and put
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-combined-Z-mark}
 \mathfrak Z_a^{\mathfrak o}:=
 1+H_a^{\mathfrak o}+Z(\mathcal G_a^{\mathfrak o})\ge1.
\end{equation}
There are $C_{\rm rec}\ge1$, $A_Z\ge0$, and $\chi_Z>0$ such that the
recovery clock obtained from the density and growth recursions satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-Z-recovery-clock}
 R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}(a)
 \le C+C_{\rm rec}\log\mathfrak Z_a^{\mathfrak o},
 \qquad
 \log\mathfrak Z_a^{\mathfrak o}
 \le C+A_Z\ell_{\rm word}(a).
\end{equation}
With $\mathcal W_a\ge1$ the common immutable coupling mark for the two
orientations, require the global recovery moment
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-global-Z-moment}
 \sup_{s,m,n,\mathfrak o}
 \int_{\{a:\mathfrak o\in\operatorname{Acc}(a)\}}
 \mathcal W_a
 (\mathfrak Z_a^{\mathfrak o})^{\chi_Z}\,
 d\overline M^q(a)<\infty.
\end{equation}
The same moment is required after normalizing each shallow collar and each
complete main/nonmain deep owner by its positive $q$-mass: in the notation
of \eqref{eq:normalized-shallow-recovery} and
\eqref{eq:normalized-deep-recovery},
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-normalized-Z-moment}
 \begin{split}
 &\sup_{s,L,\mathfrak o}
 \sum_\lambda\pi^{\rm sh}_{s,L}(\lambda)
 \mathcal W_{\lambda,s,L}^{\rm sh}
 (\mathfrak Z_{\lambda,s,L}^{\mathfrak o,\rm sh})^{\chi_Z}<\infty,\\
 &\sup_{s,L,\mathfrak o}
 \sum_\lambda\pi_{s,L}(\lambda)
 \mathcal W_{\lambda,s,L}
 (\mathfrak Z_{\lambda,s,L}^{\mathfrak o})^{\chi_Z}<\infty,
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
for the shallow and deep normalized laws respectively.  Zero-mass cells
are omitted.  Here the two clocks
$R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}$ restrict exactly to the existing
$R_{\lambda,s,L}^{\pm,\rm sh}$ and $R_{\lambda,s,L}^{\pm}$, and the two
displayed common marks are exactly the $\mathcal W$ fields in
\eqref{eq:normalized-shallow-recovery} and
\eqref{eq:normalized-deep-recovery}; no parallel recovery payload is
introduced.  Likewise
$\mathfrak Z_{\lambda,s,L}^{\mathfrak o,\rm sh}$ and
$\mathfrak Z_{\lambda,s,L}^{\mathfrak o}$ are the exact restrictions of
$\mathfrak Z_a^{\mathfrak o}$ under those same two $q$-disintegrations, so
\eqref{eq:translation-Z-recovery-clock} holds constituent by constituent
after normalization.  All integrals use the propagated $q$ law already present in
$\mathrm{TR\_MATCH}$; no family is renormalized into a new occurrence law.
Finally there are exponents $q_{\rm sh}$ and $q_{\rm deep}$ with
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-Z-rate-window}
 c_{\rm cpl}^{\rm sh}<q_{\rm sh}<\frac{\chi_Z}{C_{\rm rec}},
 \qquad
 \max\{\alpha_+,\alpha_-\}<q_{\rm deep}
 <\frac{\chi_Z}{C_{\rm rec}}.
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[One $Z$-moment formula on three prescribed laws closes all recovery ledgers]
\label{prop:translation-Z-closes-recovery}
Assume $\mathrm{TR\_Z\_REC}$ and every geometric-access, exact-current, and
positive-envelope clause of $\mathrm{TR\_FACE\_REC}$ other than the four
recovery conclusions displayed next.  Then
\eqref{eq:translation-recovery-tail},
\eqref{eq:translation-recorded-recovery-clock},
$\mathrm{SHREC}(q_{\rm sh})$, and
$\mathrm{NREC}_{\rm deep}(q_{\rm deep})$ hold.  More precisely, every
$c_R<\chi_Z/C_{\rm rec}$ is admissible after changing the constant, and
$\kappa_{\rm rec}=C_{\rm rec}A_Z$ is admissible.  This proposition does not
derive oriented access, the coarea-to-unstable entry map, either positive
mass tail, or any PPE clause.
Here ``one formula'' means the same immutable mark and exponent on the three
laws in \eqref{eq:translation-global-Z-moment}--
\eqref{eq:translation-normalized-Z-moment}; neither normalized moment is
deduced from the global one.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
From \eqref{eq:translation-Z-recovery-clock},
$\{R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}>u\}$ implies
$\mathfrak Z_a^{\mathfrak o}>
\exp((u-C)/C_{\rm rec})$.  Markov's inequality in the global moment
\eqref{eq:translation-global-Z-moment} gives
\eqref{eq:translation-recovery-tail} with every stated $c_R$.  The second
inequality in \eqref{eq:translation-Z-recovery-clock} gives the recorded
word-clock bound.  For $qC_{\rm rec}<\chi_Z$,
\[
 \mathcal W_ae^{qR_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}}
 \le C\mathcal W_a
 (\mathfrak Z_a^{\mathfrak o})^{qC_{\rm rec}},
\]
so the two normalized instances of
\eqref{eq:translation-normalized-Z-moment} give respectively
\eqref{eq:normalized-shallow-recovery} and
\eqref{eq:normalized-deep-recovery}.  The lower inequalities in
\eqref{eq:translation-Z-rate-window} are exactly the existing coupling
windows.  The density recursion of \cite[Lemma~13]{SYZ} and the normalized
boundary recursion of \cite[Lemma~16]{SYZ} furnish
\eqref{eq:translation-Z-recovery-clock} once the displayed entry family has
been constructed; they do not construct that family from an arbitrary
moving coarea face.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Unnormalized depth-stratified recovery subgate
$\mathrm{TR\_Z\_AREC}$]
\label{def:translation-Z-aggregate-recovery}
Use the same Borel entry families, immutable marks, and clocks as in
\eqref{eq:translation-combined-Z-mark}--
\eqref{eq:translation-Z-recovery-clock}, and retain the global moment
\eqref{eq:translation-global-Z-moment}.  For the shallow and complete deep
owner families require only bounded overlap on the master occurrence tree,
with the overlap multiplicity charged in the common mark $\mathcal W$.
Instead of \eqref{eq:translation-normalized-Z-moment}, require, separately
in each accessible orientation,
\begin{align}
 \sum_\lambda M^{\rm sh}_{\lambda,s,L}
  \mathcal W^{\rm sh}_{\lambda,s,L}
  (\mathfrak Z^{\mathfrak o,\rm sh}_{\lambda,s,L})^{\chi_Z}
 &\le C|s|^{-1}e^{A_{\rm sh}L},
 \label{eq:translation-aggregate-shallow-Z-moment}\\
 \sum_\lambda M_{\lambda,s,L}
  \mathcal W_{\lambda,s,L}
  (\mathfrak Z^{\mathfrak o}_{\lambda,s,L})^{\chi_Z}
 &\le C|s|^{-1}
 e^{-(c_{\rm tail}-A_{\rm loss})L}.
 \label{eq:translation-aggregate-deep-Z-moment}
\end{align}
These are restrictions of one positive occurrence measure, not expectations
under separately normalized owner laws.  The shallow and deep owner unions
and their clocks are exactly those in
\eqref{eq:aggregate-shallow-recovery} and
\eqref{eq:aggregate-deep-recovery}; no parallel disintegration is allowed.
Finally choose $q_{\rm sh}$ and $q_{\rm deep}$ in the same strict windows
\eqref{eq:translation-Z-rate-window}.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[One unnormalized $Z$ ledger closes aggregate recovery]
\label{prop:translation-aggregate-Z-closes-recovery}
Assume $\mathrm{TR\_Z\_AREC}$ and every geometric-access,
exact-current, and positive-envelope clause of
$\mathrm{TR\_FACE\_REC}$ other than the recovery conclusions.  Then
\eqref{eq:translation-recovery-tail},
\eqref{eq:translation-recorded-recovery-clock},
\eqref{eq:aggregate-shallow-recovery}, and
\eqref{eq:aggregate-deep-recovery} hold.  Thus the shallow and deep CM2
removal theorems can be invoked without a hereditary conditional moment on
every rare owner cell.  This proposition does not derive bounded owner
overlap, the coarea-to-unstable entry map, the two aggregate estimates
\eqref{eq:translation-aggregate-shallow-Z-moment}--
\eqref{eq:translation-aggregate-deep-Z-moment}, or any PPE clause.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The global moment and clock calculation in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:translation-Z-closes-recovery} give the recovery tail
and the recorded word-clock bound.  If
$qC_{\rm rec}<\chi_Z$, then $\mathfrak Z\ge1$ and
\[
 \mathcal W e^{qR_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o}}
 \le C\mathcal W\mathfrak Z^{qC_{\rm rec}}
 \le C\mathcal W\mathfrak Z^{\chi_Z}.
\]
Sum this inequality with the original owner masses.  The shallow aggregate
moment gives \eqref{eq:aggregate-shallow-recovery}, and the deep aggregate
moment gives \eqref{eq:aggregate-deep-recovery}.  Positivity permits
restriction to any owner union, while the declared overlap mark pays for
every repeated owner.  No division by an owner mass occurs.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A single level ledger is hereditary under unnormalized
restriction]
\label{lem:unnormalized-level-ledger}
Let $\nu_s$ be the positive marked occurrence measure
\[
 d\nu_s=\mathcal W\mathfrak Z^{\chi_Z}\,d\overline M_s^q
\]
on the immutable word-depth record, and suppose, for some $\gamma_Z>0$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:unnormalized-level-ledger}
 \nu_s\{\ell_{\rm word}=k\}
 \le C|s|^{-1}e^{-\gamma_Zk},\qquad k\ge0.
\end{equation}
If every shallow owner lies in $\{\ell_{\rm word}\le L\}$, every deep
owner lies in $\{\ell_{\rm word}>L\}$, and their overlap multiplicities are
included in $\mathcal W$, then
\eqref{eq:translation-aggregate-shallow-Z-moment} holds with
$A_{\rm sh}=0$ and
\eqref{eq:translation-aggregate-deep-Z-moment} holds whenever
$c_{\rm tail}-A_{\rm loss}\le\gamma_Z$.  More generally, a level bound
$C|s|^{-1}e^{-(\gamma_Z-A_Z)k}$ pays an exponential mark growth $A_Z$ and
leaves the strict tail gap $\gamma_Z-A_Z$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Bounded-overlap positivity identifies each aggregate owner sum with at most
the corresponding marked restriction of $\nu_s$.  Summing
\eqref{eq:unnormalized-level-ledger} over $k\le L$ gives the shallow bound;
summing it over $k>L$ gives the geometric deep tail.  The last statement is
the same calculation after subtracting the mark exponent before summation.
Unlike conditioning, restriction can only decrease these positive sums.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Global $L^p$ weight plus levelwise $Z$ closes the marked
depth ledger]
\label{lem:global-Lp-levelwise-Z}
For $0<|s|\le s_0\le1$, let $E_{s,k}$ be disjoint word-depth levels of one
positive occurrence law $q_s$.  Suppose, for some $c_0>0$, $p>1$, and a
nonnegative full boundary mark $X_s$,
\begin{align}
 q_s(E_{s,k})&\le C|s|^{-1}e^{-c_0k},
 \label{eq:levelwise-q-tail}\\
 \int_{E_{s,k}}X_s\,dq_s&\le Bq_s(E_{s,k}),
 \label{eq:levelwise-Z-first-moment}\\
 \int \mathcal W_s^p\,dq_s&\le K|s|^{-1}.
 \label{eq:global-level-weight-Lp}
\end{align}
Choose $0<\chi<1/p'$.  Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:derived-marked-level-ledger}
 \int_{E_{s,k}}\mathcal W_sX_s^\chi\,dq_s
 \le C_{B,K}|s|^{-1}e^{-c_0k/p'}.
\end{equation}
Consequently the shallow union has aggregate size $O(|s|^{-1})$, and the
deep union has aggregate tail
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:derived-marked-deep-tail}
 \int_{\bigcup_{k>L}E_{s,k}}
 \mathcal W_sX_s^\chi\,dq_s
 \le C_{B,K}|s|^{-1}e^{-c_0L/p'}.
\end{equation}
Thus \eqref{eq:translation-aggregate-shallow-Z-moment}--
\eqref{eq:translation-aggregate-deep-Z-moment} follow whenever the owner
unions are positive restrictions of these levels and the declared final
tail gap satisfies
$c_{\rm tail}-A_{\rm loss}<c_0/p'$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Put $r=\chi p'<1$.  Concavity on the finite measure
$q_s|_{E_{s,k}}$ and \eqref{eq:levelwise-Z-first-moment} give
\[
 \int_{E_{s,k}}X_s^r\,dq_s
 \le q_s(E_{s,k})^{1-r}
      \left(\int_{E_{s,k}}X_s\,dq_s\right)^r
 \le B^r q_s(E_{s,k}).
\]
H"older, \eqref{eq:global-level-weight-Lp}, and
\eqref{eq:levelwise-q-tail} now yield
\[
 \int_{E_{s,k}}\mathcal W_sX_s^\chi\,dq_s
 \le (K|s|^{-1})^{1/p}
      (B^rq_s(E_{s,k}))^{1/p'}
 \le C_{B,K}|s|^{-1}e^{-c_0k/p'}.
\]
Summing the geometric series below or above $L$ proves the two aggregate
bounds.  The single H"older loss occurs after registry complexity and the
unweighted depth exponent have already been aggregated; no branchwise or
ownerwise normalization is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fractional boundary mark of a measured unstable family]
\label{lem:fractional-family-boundary-mark}
Let a finite measured unstable family have component masses $p_j$, lengths
$|W_j|$, total mass $M=\sum_jp_j$, and unnormalized boundary
$Z(\mathcal G)=\sum_jp_j/|W_j|$.  For $0<r<1$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fractional-family-boundary-mark}
 \sum_jp_j|W_j|^{-r}
 \le M^{1-r}Z(\mathcal G)^r.
\end{equation}
In particular, $Z(\mathcal G)\le BM$ implies
$\sum_jp_j|W_j|^{-r}\le B^rM$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If $M=0$ there is nothing to prove.  Otherwise apply Jensen to the concave
map $x\mapsto x^r$ under the component probability $p_j/M$, with
$x=|W_j|^{-1}$.  Multiplication by $M$ gives
\eqref{eq:fractional-family-boundary-mark}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Coarea recovery supplies face-time, shallow, and deep]
\label{prop:translation-recovery-closes-three}
Under $\mathrm{TR\_FACE\_REC}$ and $\mathrm{TR\_MATCH}$, with the fixed
$\delta$ of the former,
$\mathrm{FACE\_TIME}_{\rm REC}$ holds with
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-face-time-rate}
 c_{\rm tail,fw}=\min\{\delta c_R,c_{\rm or}\},\qquad
 c_{\rm fw}^{\rm rec}\ge
 \min\{\delta c_R,c_{\rm or},
             (1-\delta)c_{\rm mix}-a_{\rm fw}\}.
\end{equation}
If, in addition, the fixed-core and boundary-tightness clauses of
$\mathrm{MT}_{\rm DQ}$ hold on the same coarea atlas, then
\eqref{eq:translation-word-tail} gives $\mathrm{FACE}_{\rm 2CUT}$.
The shallow and deep clauses give
$\mathrm{SHCOL}_{\rm env}$,
$\mathrm{TAIL}_{\rm env}^{+}$, and $\mathrm{NREC}_{\rm deep}$ with the
strict rates used by Theorems~\ref{thm:shallow-two-threshold} and
\ref{thm:time-depth-diagonal}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put $I_{R,\rm tail}\cup I_{\rm mis}$ in the positive tail and use
\eqref{eq:translation-active-recovery-tail} and
\eqref{eq:translation-orientation-mismatch-tail}.  On its complement, use
the certified long-side coupling after
$R_{\rm rec}^{\mathfrak o_*}$ iterates;
this is
\eqref{eq:face-time-recovered-correlation}.  This proves
\eqref{eq:translation-face-time-rate}.  Sum
\eqref{eq:translation-word-tail} in word depth.  The remaining conclusions
are precisely one of the two shallow/deep recovery routes and the strict
gaps in \eqref{eq:translation-recovery-windows}.  The normalized route
multiplies the small positive mass back after recovery; the aggregate route
never removes its original occurrence weight.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional three-gate interface reduction for relative translations]
\label{thm:translation-three-gate-reduction}
For the class $\mathcal Q_{\rm tr}$, the literature-backed package in
Proposition~\ref{prop:translation-literature-package}, the explicit
fixed-gauge/coarea identities of $\mathrm{MT}_{\rm DQ}$, and the three
concrete gates
\[
 \mathrm{TR\_PPE}_{\rm JET}\ \text{or}\
 \mathrm{TR\_PPE}_{\rm TERM},\qquad
 \mathrm{TR\_MATCH},\qquad
 \mathrm{TR\_FACE\_REC}
\]
verify \textup{(PPE1)}, selection-safe change of law, physical/source
matching, face-time, and the explicitly assumed shallow/deep cluster.  The
weighted-amplitude and exact mark/moment clause \textup{(PPE2)}, continuation/buffer clause
\textup{(PPE3)}, and finite-$s$ clause \textup{(PPE4)} remain additional
hypotheses for full $\mathrm{PPE}_N$; the fixed-core and boundary-tightness
clauses of $\mathrm{MT}_{\rm DQ}$ remain additional hypotheses for
$\mathrm{FACE}_{\rm 2CUT}$.  Before forming any rate margin, let
$\Theta_{\rm PPE}^{W,\rm tr}>0$ denote the exponent actually proved by the
weighted parentwise clause \textup{(PPE2)} and
$\mathrm{TENERGY}_{W}$ on these same frozen parents.  If it is obtained from
the raw exponent $\Theta_{\rm PPE}^{\rm tr}$ in
\eqref{eq:translation-ppe-exponent} or
\eqref{eq:translation-terminal-ppe-exponent} only through
$\mathrm{KRH}_{p_W}$, then one may use no exponent larger than
\begin{equation}
 \Theta_{\rm PPE}^{W,\rm tr}
 \le \Theta_{\rm PPE}^{\rm tr}/p_W'.
\label{eq:translation-weighted-ppe-exponent}
\end{equation}
A direct weighted sublevel theorem may provide a sharper displayed value.
Without a verified positive $\Theta_{\rm PPE}^{W,\rm tr}$ the reduction stops
here, even if the raw jet gate holds.  Let
$c_{{\rm ref},{\rm PPE}}^{\rm tr}$ denote the \emph{full}
minimum in \eqref{eq:explicit-reference-energy-rates} after inserting the
weighted exponent $\Theta_{\rm PPE}^{W,\rm tr}$, rather than the raw exponent,
and every remaining
context, stopping, buffer, gate, continuation, and probability-clock rate.
Set
$a_L^N:=a_{{\rm main},{\rm PPE}}^L$ and
$a_B^N:=a_{{\rm main},{\rm PPE}}^B$, the actual PPE-row likelihood and
base losses per collision time in
\eqref{eq:selection-safe-likelihood-rates} and
\eqref{eq:main-source-base-mass}, and use the same $p_{\rm joint}'$ as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-source-energy-lift}.  Independently, on the parameter
clock $t_s:=\log(1/|s|)$, put
$L(s)=\lfloor\kappa_Lt_s\rfloor$ and
$A_D:=A_{\rm loss}$ from \eqref{eq:translation-deep-tail}.  The exact new
gaps are
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:translation-three-new-margins}
\begin{split}
 c_{\rm main}^{\rm tr}
 &=\frac12\left\{\frac{c_{{\rm ref},{\rm PPE}}^{\rm tr}}
                         {p_{\rm joint}'}
                  -a_L^N-a_B^N\right\}>0,\\
 c_{\rm face}^{\rm tr}
 &=\frac12\min\{\delta c_R,c_{\rm or},
                  (1-\delta)c_{\rm mix}-a_{\rm fw}\}>0,\\
 c_{\rm deep,cut}^{\rm tr}
 &=\kappa_L(c_{\rm tail}-A_D)-1>0.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
Here every symbol is measured on the same physical/source occurrence law;
none is a parallel unweighted word count.  In particular an exceptional
PPE rate smaller than the finite-type good-set rate remains inside
$c_{{\rm ref},{\rm PPE}}^{\rm tr}$, and no per-time likelihood loss is
rescaled by the independent parameter cutoff.  The first two lines use collision
time $N=\max\{m,n\}$; the last uses the independent parameter clock $t_s$.
The last line is the finite-$s$
difference-quotient cutoff exponent after the explicit $|s|^{-1}$ loss; it
is not a product-time mixing rate.  If their exponents satisfy
\eqref{eq:translation-ppe-positive-window} in the direct-jet case or
\eqref{eq:translation-terminal-positive-window} in the terminal-gate case,
together with \eqref{eq:translation-recovery-windows} and all remaining strict gaps in
\eqref{eq:complete-phase-exponent} and
\eqref{eq:final-CM2-exponent}, and if \emph{all} remaining hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:global-cm2-assembly} are verified on the same base incidence
kernel, then that genuinely moving relative-translation path satisfies CM2.
Those remaining hypotheses include $\mathrm{QT}_{\rm path}$,
$\mathrm{UCR}_{\rm sh}$, $\mathrm{FZ}_{\rm geom}$,
$\mathrm{SF}_{\rm split}$, $\mathrm{CP}_{\rm env}$, the full convergence
and regular/face clauses of $\mathrm{MT}_{\rm DQ}$, and every other
probability, response, and finite marked-tree input listed there.  The three
gates do not prove those conditions and this theorem is an interface
reduction, not a nonvacuity theorem.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Exact current frontier]
\label{rem:translation-research-frontier}
No cited theorem currently proves the three displayed gates simultaneously
for the actual collision--SRB descendants of a moving dispersing billiard.
The Fourier-decay results \cite{LPS,Leclerc2026} concern smooth
Axiom--A/conformal or parabolic iterated systems and do not supply
\eqref{eq:translation-ppe-derivative-gate} through billiard homogeneity
cuts.  The terminal alternative lowers the accumulated jet and component
losses, but still needs the actual-SRB block hazard, stability after
miss-conditioning, and the native-coordinate rough transfer in
Remark~\ref{rem:translation-terminal-rough-transfer}.  The random-scatterer model of \cite{StenlundRandom} and the random
Lorentz-gas local limit theorem \cite{DemersPeneZhang} suggest an annealed
route: condition on all but one refreshed center coordinate and prove a
one-block coarea derivative bound.  Concretely, suppose an active refreshed
coordinate $c_j$, with conditional density bounded above and below, is
independent of the frozen opposite context, native chart, and coefficients
of every admissible $P$, and combine this with the one-block derivative
bound on $\partial_{c_j}X^{[N]}$.  More generally, without that independence,
require directly and uniformly in the admissible polynomials that there are
at most $e^{a_{\rm env,cmp}N}$ monotonicity pieces and
\[
 |\partial_{c_j}(X^{[N]}-P)|\ge e^{-a_{\rm env}N}
\]
outside joint environment--SRB mass $Ce^{-b_{\rm env}N}$.  Conditional
coarea then gives
\[
 (\mathbb P\otimes\mu_Q^u)\{|X^{[N]}-P|\le r\}
 \le C r e^{(a_{\rm env}+a_{\rm env,cmp})N}
      +Ce^{-b_{\rm env}N}.
\]
Thus $N=C_{\rm buf}\log(1/r)$ gives a positive annealed exponent if
$C_{\rm buf}(a_{\rm env}+a_{\rm env,cmp})<1$.  A quenched conclusion still
requires weighted Borel--Cantelli for the bad descendant mass, not a union
bound over all symbolic words.  This would be a useful testbed, but it does
not by itself prove the autonomous pathwise theorem above.  Likewise,
\cite{CanestrariHoles} proves conditional mixing only after the shrinking
boundary source is shown to be a regular standard family.  The obstruction
is not cosmetic: the 2026 review \cite[Problem~8.7]{DLreview} records that
small-set indicators can concentrate densities on atypical trajectories,
and general aperiodic Lorentz-gas memory loss remains open.  The main new
geometric work is therefore sharply localized: prove the uniform
depth-$N$ derivative gate or its terminal-gate replacement, the
occurrencewise coarea holonomy density, and
the recovery/tail estimates on one common relative-translation refinement.
In particular, one should not try to verify
$\mathrm{FACE\_TIME}_{\rm CM2}$ by showing that the entire positive face
mass decays; the recovered-core alternative is the nonvacuous route.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-phase abstract CM2 assembly]
\label{thm:fixed-phase-cm2-assembly}
Let $F_s:M\to M$ be an analytic one-parameter family of invertible
piecewise-$C^{r+1}$ maps on one fixed finite-component collision section,
preserving one probability $\mu$.  Define the target correlations, transfer
operators, first difference-quotient defect, observables, and CM2 norm directly
for $F_s$.  Suppose the complete regular/face/product-current/response
decomposition and every typed interface in \textup{(H1)--(H5)} of
Theorem~\ref{thm:global-cm2-assembly} are instantiated for this very family
$F_s$ on one common occurrence tree, with the same strict exponent windows.
Then the fixed-section response satisfies CM2.

This theorem changes the quantified object: it is a theorem about the maps
