$F_s$ themselves, not an assertion that an induced section is a standard
full-boundary collision map.  It therefore gives no CM2 statement for a
different full collision map unless a separate section-response transfer
theorem identifies the two targets.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
After the measure-trivialized target and first-defect decomposition have been
fixed, the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:global-cm2-assembly} uses only the
operator identities, occurrence/source commuting diagram, probability and
source clocks, slope-energy estimate, recovery bounds, and the two
difference-quotient convergence topologies explicitly listed in
\textup{(H1)--(H5)}.  Apply that proof to the directly instantiated operators
of $F_s$.  No inducing identity is used in this argument, so the conclusion
is exactly the fixed-section CM2 conclusion and nothing more.
\end{proof}

\section{A fixed-section two-disk certification target}

The relative-translation programme above still ranges over many geometries.
We now freeze one explicit nontrivial model and separate what is already an
exact theorem from the genuinely new all-depth certificates.  This prevents
``small perturbation'' from serving as a surrogate for nonvacuity.

\begin{proposition}[Exact geometry and common law for the fixed-section pilot]
\label{prop:fixed-section-exact-geometry}
Take the two-disk model of \cite{StenlundRandom} with fixed gray radius
\begin{equation}
 \bar R=\frac9{25},\qquad R=\frac4{25},\qquad
 \varepsilon=\frac1{200}.
\label{eq:fixed-section-rational-data}
\end{equation}
Then its elementary fixed-section geometry windows hold strictly:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-geometry-windows}
 \max\{\bar R,R+\varepsilon\}<\frac12,
 \quad (\bar R+R+\varepsilon)^2<\frac12,
 \quad \bar R^2>\frac18,
 \quad \bar R+R-\varepsilon>\frac12.
\end{equation}
The corresponding margins are respectively
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-geometry-margins}
 0.14,\quad
 2^{-1/2}-0.525>0.18210,\quad
 0.36-(2\sqrt2)^{-1}>0.00644,\quad
 0.015.
\end{equation}
Moreover the free-zone threshold in that construction is
\begin{equation}
 L(\bar R)=\frac{5\sqrt7}{32}-\frac{63}{800}>\frac{33}{200}
 =R+\varepsilon,
\label{eq:fixed-section-free-zone}
\end{equation}
with margin greater than $0.16964$.

For every admissible white-center displacement $c$, the return map $F_c$
acts on one common fixed collision section $M$, is invertible with
$F_c^{-1}=I\circ F_c\circ I$, and preserves the same probability
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal N_M:=\int_M\cos\varphi\,dr\,d\varphi,
 \qquad
 d\mu=\mathcal N_M^{-1}\cos\varphi\,dr\,d\varphi.
\label{eq:fixed-section-common-law}
\end{equation}
Each return meets the moving white disk at most once.  Hence on the physical
fixed-section dynamics the unconditioned carrier Radon--Nikodym factor is
exactly one; this is not merely a uniform comparison.  No assertion is made
here about the Jacobian of a moving-face coarea restriction or a normalized
source proposal.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first and fourth inequalities in
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-geometry-windows} are rational arithmetic.  The
second is equivalent to $(21/40)^2<1/2$, and the third to
$(9/25)^2>1/8$.  For \eqref{eq:fixed-section-free-zone}, it is enough to
show $25\sqrt7>39$, which follows from $625\cdot7>39^2$.
The sharper numerical margin in \eqref{eq:fixed-section-geometry-margins}
is also exact: after collecting rational terms it is equivalent to
$\sqrt7>41339/15625$, whose square has slack
$7-(41339/15625)^2=71454/244140625>0$.
The fixed-section,
invariance, reversibility, and one-white-hit assertions are the construction
of \cite[Sections~2.1--2.4]{StenlundRandom}.  Notice that this proves an
exact discrete moving-scatterer cocycle statement; it does not manufacture
a continuous-time moving-table flow.
\end{proof}

Fix a nonzero translation vector $v_c$ and a center $c_0$ in the certified
free zone.  Choose $s_0>0$ so that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{|s|\le s_0}|c_0+sv_c|\le\varepsilon,
\label{eq:fixed-section-path-admissibility}
\end{equation}
and define the deterministic analytic path
\begin{equation}
 c(s)=c_0+sv_c,\qquad |s|\le s_0.
\label{eq:fixed-section-deterministic-path}
\end{equation}
For the explicit positive-mass cylinder below we take
$c_0=0$, $v_c=e_1$, and $s_0=1/400$; hence
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-path-admissibility} holds with strict slack.
This, rather than the refreshed random cocycle, is the pilot to which the
pathwise CM2 theorem can be applied.

\begin{proposition}[Exact two-step inducing identity]
\label{prop:fixed-section-two-step-inducing}
Let $M^*=M\sqcup W$ be Stenlund's enlarged section, where $W$ is the white-disk
collision component in the fixed boundary parametrization, and let
$\mathcal P_c^*$ be the transfer operator of $F_c^*$ on finite signed measures
or on any common distribution pair on which the following restrictions and
compositions are bounded.  Relative to the decomposition $M\sqcup W$, write
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal P_c^*=\begin{pmatrix}A_c&B_c\\ C_c&0\end{pmatrix}.
\label{eq:fixed-section-enlarged-block-operator}
\end{equation}
Here $A_c$ is the direct $M\to M$ branch, $C_c$ the $M\to W$ first white-hit
branch, and $B_c$ the subsequent $W\to M$ branch.  Then the transfer operator
$\mathcal P_c$ of the fixed-section first-return map $F_c$ is exactly
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal P_c=A_c+B_cC_c.
\label{eq:fixed-section-induced-operator}
\end{equation}
Consequently, along any admissible path and for every $s\ne0$,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\mathcal P_{c(s)}-\mathcal P_{c(0)}}s
 =\frac{A_{c(s)}-A_{c(0)}}s
  +\frac{B_{c(s)}-B_{c(0)}}sC_{c(0)}
  +B_{c(s)}\frac{C_{c(s)}-C_{c(0)}}s.
\label{eq:fixed-section-induced-DQ}
\end{equation}
The identity reduces convergence of the induced first defect to three block
quotients together with a topology that permits the moving composition
$B_{c(s)}\Delta_sC$.  Separate weak convergence of the two factors is not
enough; the exact test-side condition is isolated below.  There is no
infinite return-time series and no derivative of an unrecorded stopping time.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By the free-zone property, a point of $M$ either returns directly to $M$ or
first enters $W$ and then returns to $M$.  A point of $W$ returns to $M$ at
its next $F_c^*$ iterate, so the $W\to W$ block is zero.  Splitting the dual
pairing according to these two disjoint possibilities gives
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-induced-operator}.  Add and subtract
$B_{c(s)}C_{c(0)}$ to obtain
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-induced-DQ}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Typed block-current certificate
$\mathrm{BLOCK\_DQ}_{M,W}$]
\label{def:fixed-section-block-DQ-certificate}
For $X\in\{M,W\}$ let
$\mathfrak B_{X,2}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak B_{X,1}$ be fixed strong/current
source spaces and let $\mathfrak T_X$ be a fixed separating test space, with
$|\langle u,\phi\rangle_X|\le
\|u\|_{\mathfrak B_{X,1}}\|\phi\|_{\mathfrak T_X}$.
The block certificate consists of the following data, all recorded before a
final test or a depth query is chosen.  All blocks are raw and written in the
same block-diagonal gauge; no intermediate centering is inserted on $W$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(BD\arabic*)}]
\item The blocks in
      \eqref{eq:fixed-section-enlarged-block-operator} extend to every
      composition below, with
      \[
       C_0\in\mathcal L(\mathfrak B_{M,2},\mathfrak B_{W,2}),
       \qquad
       B_s\in\mathcal L(\mathfrak B_{W,1},\mathfrak T_M^*).
      \]
      There are adjoint test actions
      $\langle B_su,\phi\rangle_M=\langle u,B_s^*\phi\rangle_W$ satisfying
      \begin{equation}
      \label{eq:fixed-section-block-test-continuity}
       \sup_{|s|<s_0}\|B_s^*\|_{\mathfrak T_M\to\mathfrak T_W}<\infty,
       \qquad
       \|(B_s^*-B_0^*)\phi\|_{\mathfrak T_W}\longrightarrow0
       \quad(\phi\in\mathfrak T_M).
      \end{equation}
      Thus weak convergence of $\Delta_sC$ is never multiplied by a second
      merely weak or pointwise convergent factor.
\item The three quotient families are uniformly bounded in
      \begin{equation}
      \label{eq:fixed-section-block-DQ-uniform-bounds}
      \begin{split}
       \Delta_sA&:\mathfrak B_{M,2}\to\mathfrak T_M^*,\qquad
       \Delta_sB:\mathfrak B_{W,2}\to\mathfrak T_M^*,\\
       \Delta_sC&:\mathfrak B_{M,2}\to\mathfrak B_{W,1},
      \end{split}
      \end{equation}
      and, on one common recorded branch atlas, there are bilinear currents
      $J_A,J_B,J_C$ such that
      \begin{align}
       \langle\Delta_sAh,\phi\rangle_M&\longrightarrow J_A(h,\phi),
       &\langle\Delta_sBk,\phi\rangle_M&\longrightarrow J_B(k,\phi),
       \label{eq:fixed-section-block-DQ-limits}\\
       \langle\Delta_sCh,\psi\rangle_W&\longrightarrow J_C(h,\psi).
       \nonumber
      \end{align}
      The convergence includes the common-atlas boundary tightness needed to
      test against branchwise observables; a fixed-interior-core limit is not
      called the full block limit.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Block occurrence certificate
$\mathrm{BLOCK\_MATCH}_{M,W}$]
\label{def:fixed-section-block-match-certificate}
The block-matching certificate is independent of
$\mathrm{BLOCK\_DQ}_{M,W}$ and consists of the following data.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(BM\arabic*)}]
\item The physical occurrence space is the disjoint tagged union
      \begin{equation}
      \label{eq:fixed-section-block-occurrence-tags}
       \Omega_{\rm block}^{\rm phys}
       =\Omega_{\rm dir}^{\circ}\sqcup\Omega_{\rm ent}^{\circ}
        \sqcup\Omega_{\rm exit}^{\circ}\sqcup\Omega_{\partial}.
      \end{equation}
      Before a trace identity is proved,
      $\Omega_{\partial}=\Omega_{A,\partial}\sqcup
      \Omega_{C,\partial}$ consists of two distinct additive occurrences and
      both are charged.  After their common physical coefficient and traces
      have been identified, those two tags are removed and
      $\Omega_{\partial}$ is replaced by the single assembled jump
      occurrence; if its trace jump is zero, it is the null occurrence.
      The pre-trace pair and the post-trace jump never coexist in one ledger.
      Each tag carries
      its exact positive coefficient kernel $D_\omega^m$, polarity, carrier,
      current operator, component index, and boundary chart.
\item The source occurrence space has the identical tags and records.  One
      record-preserving Markov kernel pushes the exact physical $m$-measure
      to the source $m$-measure, integrates the same scalar current for every
      test, and pushes the physical propagated $q$-envelope to a dominating
      source envelope.  Thus the restriction of
      \eqref{eq:prop-q-exact-m-match}--
      \eqref{eq:propagated-q-physical-match} to every block tag holds before
      the tags are summed.  Separate sectionwise choices of $q$ do not
      constitute this clause.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Typed block product rule]
\label{prop:fixed-section-typed-block-DQ}
Assume $\mathrm{BLOCK\_DQ}_{M,W}$.  Then the fixed-section quotient converges
in the fixed weak operator topology and its limit is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-block-DQ-limit-product-rule}
 J_{\mathcal P}(h,\phi)
 =J_A(h,\phi)+J_B(C_0h,\phi)+J_C(h,B_0^*\phi).
\end{equation}
Equivalently,
$J_{\mathcal P}=J_{\rm dir}+J_{\rm exit}+J_{\rm ent}$ with
$J_{\rm dir}=J_A$, $J_{\rm exit}=J_BC_0$, and
$J_{\rm ent}=(B_0)_\#J_C$.  This identity is raw.  Only after all three
currents and their boundary traces have been assembled is the fixed-$M$
centering $\Pi_{M,0}^{\perp}J_{\mathcal P}\Pi_{M,0}^{\perp}$ applied; no
$W$-centering is inserted between $B_0$ and $J_C$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Pair \eqref{eq:fixed-section-induced-DQ} with $(h,\phi)$.  Only the entry
term contains two moving objects, and
\[
 \langle B_s\Delta_sCh,\phi\rangle_M
 =\langle\Delta_sCh,B_s^*\phi\rangle_W.
\]
Add and subtract $B_0^*\phi$.  The fixed-test term converges to
$J_C(h,B_0^*\phi)$ by \textup{(BD2)}, while the remainder tends to zero by
the uniform $\mathfrak B_{W,1}$ bound in
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-DQ-uniform-bounds} and the strong test-side
continuity \eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-test-continuity}.  The other two
terms converge directly, proving
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-DQ-limit-product-rule}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Weak--weak block gluing is false]
\label{prop:fixed-section-weak-block-countermodel}
Uniform bounds and separate weak convergence of the three block quotients do
not imply \eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-DQ-limit-product-rule} without
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-test-continuity}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $\mathfrak B_{M,2}=\mathfrak T_M=\mathbb R$,
$\mathfrak B_{W,1}=\mathfrak T_W=\ell^2$, and
$\mathfrak B_{W,2}=\{x:\sum_k k^2|x_k|^2<\infty\}$ with its weighted norm.
For $0<|s|<1/2$ put $N(s)=\lfloor|s|^{-1}\rfloor$ and set
$C_0=B_0=0$,
\[
 C_sh=sh e_{N(s)},
 \qquad B_sx=\langle x,e_{N(s)}\rangle.
\]
Then $\Delta_sCh=he_{N(s)}\rightharpoonup0$ in $\ell^2$, while
$\Delta_sBx=s^{-1}x_{N(s)}\to0$ for every $x\in\mathfrak B_{W,2}$.
The latter functionals are uniformly bounded because
$|s|^{-1}/N(s)\le2$.  Nevertheless
$B_s\Delta_sCh=h$.  Here $B_s^*1=e_{N(s)}$ does not converge strongly
in the fixed $W$-test norm, exactly violating
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-test-continuity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Diagonal block-occurrence realization]
\label{cor:fixed-section-diagonal-block-match}
After every proved artificial trace cancellation, suppose the three tagged
physical block currents admit query-independent jointly Borel MPD kernels
$D_{\omega,\beta}^m$, $\beta\in\{{\rm dir},{\rm exit},{\rm ent}\}$, on a
standard-Borel occurrence space, and predictable envelope densities
$\mathscr C_{\rm occ}^{\rm phys}\ge1$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-block-physical-envelope}
 \int\mathscr C_{\rm occ}^{\rm phys}(\upsilon)
      \,D_\omega^m(d\upsilon)\le\mathscr W_*(\omega)
\end{equation}
on every record restriction.  Assume the charged physical/tree factor is
included before every master query.

Define the source incidence graph as a record-preserving copy of this
physical occurrence space, copy $(\nu,U)$ and every immutable field, and let
the occurrence kernel be the Dirac kernel on that copy.  Define $m$ as the
pushforward of the charged physical coefficient measure and $q$ as the
pushforward after multiplication by
$\mathscr C_{\rm occ}^{\rm phys}$.  Then the compatibility graph has
conditional mass one,
\eqref{eq:prop-q-exact-m-match} and the scalar-current identity hold exactly,
and \eqref{eq:propagated-q-physical-match} holds with constant one.  Hence
this construction verifies the block restriction of
$\mathrm{PROP\_Q\_MATCH}$ and $\mathrm{BLOCK\_MATCH}_{M,W}$ rather than
assuming an independently scaled source law.  Zero-mass parents use the
fixed null record.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The fixed copy map is a Borel isomorphism on each disjoint block tag.  Its
Dirac kernel preserves the complete record and is Markov.  Pushforward gives
the exact $m$ identity, and copying $(\nu,U)$ gives the same scalar integrand
after every record-measurable restriction.  Since
$\mathscr C_{\rm occ}^{\rm phys}\ge1$, one has $m\le q$; pushing
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-physical-envelope} forward gives the propagated
$q$ domination with constant one.
\end{proof}

The next lemma isolates what can be proved on a persistent regular branch
without assuming the all-depth boundary atlas.

\begin{lemma}[Clean-core transport and switching currents]
\label{lem:fixed-section-clean-core-block-current}
Let $X,Y$ be fixed compact coordinate charts with smooth reference measure
$\lambda$ on $X$.  Suppose $f_s:X\to Y$ is $C^2$ jointly in $(s,x)$ on a
persistent compact core $K\Subset X$, and put
$V_f(x)=\partial_sf_s(x)|_{s=0}$.  For $h\in L^1(\lambda)$ supported in $K$,
\begin{align}
 \frac{(f_s)_*(h\lambda)-(f_0)_*(h\lambda)}s
 &\longrightarrow \mathcal T_{f,h}
 &&\hbox{weakly in }(C^1(Y))^*,
 \label{eq:clean-core-transport-current}\\
 \langle\mathcal T_{f,h},\phi\rangle
 &=\int_Kh(x)D\phi(f_0x)V_f(x)\,d\lambda(x).
 \label{eq:clean-core-transport-pairing}
\end{align}
The convergence is in the norm of $(C^2(Y))^*$ when the $C^2$ norms of the
branch and its $s$-derivatives are bounded on $K$.

More generally, let $H_s$ be $C^2$, let
$D_s^{\rm hit}=\{H_s<0\}$ and
$D_s^{\rm dir}=\{H_s>0\}$, and assume
$|\nabla_xH_0|\ge c_H>0$ on the regular switching face
$\Sigma_0=\{H_0=0\}$.  For this moving-domain assertion additionally assume
$h\in C^0(X)$ (more generally, a declared source with a bounded coarea trace
on $\Sigma_0$).  Write $d\lambda=\rho_\lambda dx$ in the chart and define
$d\sigma_\lambda:=\rho_\lambda\,d\mathcal H^{\dim X-1}$ on $\Sigma_0$.
If $f_s^{\rm dir}$ and $f_s^{\rm hit}$ are the two persistent branch maps
into the same target, then the derivative of
\[
 (f_s^{\rm dir})_*(\one_{D_s^{\rm dir}}h\lambda)
 +(f_s^{\rm hit})_*(\one_{D_s^{\rm hit}}h\lambda)
\]
is the sum of the two interior currents in
\eqref{eq:clean-core-transport-current} and the single switching current
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:clean-core-switching-current}
 \int_{\Sigma_0}h(x)
 \{\phi(f_0^{\rm dir}x)-\phi(f_0^{\rm hit}x)\}
 \frac{\partial_sH_0(x)}{|\nabla_xH_0(x)|}
 \,d\sigma_\lambda(x).
\end{equation}
Grazing points, corners, and endpoints where the displayed lower bound fails
are not covered by this formula.  In a distribution source space the trace
norm used here must be included explicitly in the block certificate; an
arbitrary $L^1$ representative has no such trace.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For the fixed core, pair the quotient with $\phi$ and apply the fundamental
theorem of calculus to $\phi(f_sx)$.  Dominated convergence gives the weak
limit, while Taylor's formula gives the $(C^2)^*$ norm statement.  For the
moving domains, the coarea/Reynolds formula gives boundary normal velocity
$-\partial_sH_0/|\nabla_xH_0|$ for the hit side and its negative for the
direct side.  Adding the two boundary terms gives
\eqref{eq:clean-core-switching-current}.  This is one jump current; splitting
it into two unrelated positive coefficient laws would lose the exact
physical scale.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Regular tangency trace cancellation]
\label{cor:fixed-section-tangency-trace-cancellation}
On a regular switching chart for the fixed-section billiard, suppose
$H_s=0$ means that the flight is tangent to the white disk, with no
simultaneous collision, corner, or lift change before the next return to
$M$.  Let $c_s^{\rm br}$ be the hit-side branch map from $M$ to $W$ and
$b_s^{\rm br}$ its subsequent branch map from $W$ to $M$.  Assume the direct
branch and $b_0^{\rm br}\circ c_0^{\rm br}$ admit continuous one-sided traces
in compactified collision charts at the regular tangency face.  Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-tangency-trace-identity}
 \operatorname{Tr}_{\rm dir}f_0^{\rm dir}
 =\operatorname{Tr}_{\rm hit}
   (b_0^{\rm br}\circ c_0^{\rm br})
 \quad\hbox{on }\Sigma_0.
\end{equation}
Consequently the artificial boundary current in $\dot A$ cancels exactly
with the $B_0$-pushforward of the corresponding domain-boundary current in
$\dot C$.  The combined induced current has no independently weighted
switching occurrence on this regular chart.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
At tangency the incoming velocity is orthogonal to the collision normal, so
the specular reflection formula leaves it unchanged.  The tangent contact
therefore has the same limiting position--velocity continuation as the
direct flight, and the assumed one-sided return-chart continuity gives the
same limiting next return to $M$.  This proves
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-tangency-trace-identity}; no transfer-operator block
is applied to a grazing boundary point.  Insert that identity into the jump in
\eqref{eq:clean-core-switching-current}.  The jump vanishes pointwise, which
is precisely the cancellation of the two separately generated block
boundary terms after the $B_0$ trace is taken.  This statement does not
control the blow-up of nearby interior derivatives as grazing is approached.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Square-root gluing gives an integrable assembled current]
\label{lem:square-root-gluing-current}
Let $X$ be a compact coordinate chart with bounded smooth density $a(x)dx$,
let $H_s\in C^2((-s_0,s_0)\times X)$, and assume
$|\nabla_xH_s|\ge c_H>0$ on $\{|H_s|\le h_0\}$.  Put
$\delta_s=(H_s)_+^{1/2}$.  Suppose two branch maps into one fixed target
chart satisfy
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:square-root-branch-gluing}
 f_s^{\rm hit}(x)=f_s^{\rm dir}(x)
 +\delta_s(x)G(s,x,\delta_s(x))
 \quad\text{on }\{H_s>0\},
\end{equation}
where $f^{\rm dir}$ and $G$ have bounded $C^1$ norms on the compactified
chart.  Define the assembled push-forward
\[
 \nu_s=(f_s^{\rm dir})_*(\one_{\{H_s\le0\}}a\,dx)
       +(f_s^{\rm hit})_*(\one_{\{H_s>0\}}a\,dx).
\]
Then $(\nu_s-\nu_0)/s$ converges weakly in $(C^1)^*$ to a finite current.
For every $\phi\in C^1$ its near-face part has the uniform bound
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:square-root-current-envelope}
 \left|\langle\dot\nu_{\{0<H_0<\epsilon^2\}},\phi\rangle\right|
 \le C\epsilon\|a\|_\infty\|\phi\|_{C^1},
 \qquad 0<\epsilon<\sqrt{h_0}.
\end{equation}
The analogous estimate holds uniformly for finite-$s$ difference quotients
after enlarging the collar by $C|s|$.  In particular the assembled current
is tight at the regular switching face and has no boundary Dirac mass.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Pair with $\phi$ and write the exact assembled expression as
\begin{multline*}
 \langle\nu_s,\phi\rangle
 =\int_Xa(x)\phi(f_s^{\rm dir}x)\,dx\\
 +\int_Xa(x)\one_{\{H_s>0\}}
 \{\phi(f_s^{\rm hit}x)-\phi(f_s^{\rm dir}x)\}\,dx.
\end{multline*}
By \eqref{eq:square-root-branch-gluing} and the fundamental theorem of
calculus, the second integrand is
\[
 (H_s)_+^{1/2}K_\phi\bigl(s,x,(H_s)_+^{1/2}\bigr),
\]
where
$|K_\phi|+|\partial_sK_\phi|\le C\|\phi\|_{C^1}$.  Its almost-everywhere
$s$-derivative is bounded by
\[
 C\|\phi\|_{C^1}
 \left(1+\one_{\{H_s>0\}}H_s^{-1/2}\right).
\]
The coarea formula and the uniform regular-level bound give
\[
 \int_{\{0<H_s<\epsilon^2\}}H_s^{-1/2}\,dx
 \le C\int_0^{\epsilon^2}t^{-1/2}\,dt\le C\epsilon.
\]
Dominated differentiation proves the weak derivative and
\eqref{eq:square-root-current-envelope}.  Applying the same calculation to
the segment $\theta s$, $0\le\theta\le1$, gives the finite-$s$ envelope.
Since $(H_s)_+^{1/2}$ has zero trace at $H_s=0$, distributional
differentiation creates no boundary Dirac mass.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Circular regular tangencies have square-root gluing]
\label{prop:fixed-section-square-root-tangency}
Consider a fixed-section switching chart in which an incoming free flight
from $(q,u)$ is tangent to the moving white circle of radius $R$, with no
simultaneous collision, corner, phase endpoint, or lift change, and with the
next return to $M$ transverse.  Write
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:circular-tangency-discriminant}
 p_s=(q-c(s))\cdot u,qquad
 b_s=q-c(s)-p_su,qquad
 H_s=R^2-|b_s|^2.
\end{equation}
On the hit side put $\delta_s=\sqrt{H_s}$.  The first impact time, normal,
and reflected velocity are exactly
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:circular-tangency-root-reflection}
 t_s=-p_s-\delta_s,qquad
 n_s=R^{-1}(b_s-\delta_su),qquad
 u_s^+=u+2R^{-1}\delta_sn_s.
\end{equation}
Consequently the complete hit return and direct return satisfy
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-square-root-gluing}
 f_s^{\rm hit}=f_s^{\rm dir}
 +\delta_sG(s,x,\delta_s)
\end{equation}
with bounded $C^1$ function $G$ on every compact regular tangency chart.
Hence Lemma~\ref{lem:square-root-gluing-current} proves the genuine
near-grazing interior-current integrability and boundary tightness there.
Every compact subset of the regular tangency locus admits a finite cover
with uniform constants.  The only remaining switching-boundary pieces are
neighbourhoods of simultaneous collisions, phase endpoints, corners, and
lift changes.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The line-circle equation is
$|q+tu-c(s)|^2=R^2$.  Completing the square gives the two roots
$-p_s\pm\sqrt{R^2-|b_s|^2}$; the first root and its normal are the first two
identities in \eqref{eq:circular-tangency-root-reflection}.  Since
$u\cdot n_s=-\delta_s/R$, specular reflection gives the third identity.
At $\delta_s=0$ the contact point lies on the direct flight and reflection
does not change the velocity.  The impact point and outgoing velocity
therefore differ from their tangency continuations by $O(\delta_s)$, with a
bounded smooth quotient in $(s,x,\delta_s)$.  The next-return map is smooth
on the assumed transverse chart, so Taylor expansion through that map gives
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-square-root-gluing}.  Compactness gives a finite
subcover and uniform constants.  The excluded multiple-event sets are
precisely where this single discriminant chart or the transverse return map
ceases to be valid.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Regular tangency has a finite positive current envelope]
\label{cor:fixed-section-tangency-positive-envelope}
On every compact regular tangency chart of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-square-root-tangency}, the assembled
current admits a finite coordinate-polarity decomposition whose common
positive occurrence envelope is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-tangency-positive-envelope}
 dq_{\rm tan}(x)=Ca(x)
 \left(1+\one_{\{H_0>0\}}H_0(x)^{-1/2}\right)dx.
\end{equation}
It has finite mass and the tight collar estimate
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-tangency-envelope-tightness}
 q_{\rm tan}\{0<H_0<\epsilon^2\}\le C\epsilon.
\end{equation}
Copying each coordinate and polarity tag by the identity occurrence kernel
gives the exact physical/source scalar-current identity and $m\le q_{\rm tan}$
on every record restriction.  Thus the regular tangency subatlas verifies
the local positive-envelope and diagonal matching parts of
\textup{(FS1c)}; its attachment to the pre-existing complete tree remains a
global record/Radon--Nikodym task.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:square-root-gluing-current} writes the derivative
as a target-gradient pairing with a vector density bounded by the density in
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-tangency-positive-envelope}.  Split each target
coordinate of that vector into its positive and negative parts.  This gives
finitely many constant-polarity tags, all dominated by the same $q_{\rm tan}$.
Finiteness and \eqref{eq:fixed-section-tangency-envelope-tightness} are the
coarea estimate \eqref{eq:square-root-current-envelope}.  Identity copying
preserves the signed coefficient, positive envelope, carrier, and scalar
test pairing on every restriction.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Two transverse square-root events remain integrable]
\label{lem:two-root-integrability}
Let $H_1,H_2\in C^1(X)$ on a two-dimensional chart and assume
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:two-root-transversality}
 |\det D(H_1,H_2)(x)|\ge c_{12}>0
 \quad\text{on }\{|H_1|+|H_2|\le h_0\}.
\end{equation}
Then every positive density bounded on the chart integrates the envelope
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:two-root-envelope}
 1+(H_1)_+^{-1/2}+(H_2)_+^{-1/2}
   +(H_1H_2)_+^{-1/2}.
\end{equation}
Moreover the mass of this envelope in
$\{0<H_i<\epsilon_i^2, i=1,2\}$ is $O(\epsilon_1\epsilon_2+epsilon_1+epsilon_2)$
and tends to zero with $(\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2)$.  Thus a composition of two
transverse circular root charts has a tight first-parameter current even
when the chain rule produces the product singularity in
\eqref{eq:two-root-envelope}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By \eqref{eq:two-root-transversality}, $(H_1,H_2)$ is a uniformly regular
coordinate map after shrinking the chart.  Change variables to
$(t_1,t_2)=(H_1,H_2)$.  The four terms reduce to products of the elementary
integrals of $1$ and $t^{-1/2}$ on $(0,\epsilon_i^2)$, all finite and with
the stated vanishing collar bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite event-atlas reduction for the fixed section]
\label{prop:fixed-section-finite-event-atlas}
For the rational model \eqref{eq:fixed-section-rational-data} and an
admissible analytic center path, the one-return singularity set has a
uniformly bounded finite number of piecewise smooth branches.  Away from
their endpoints and intersections, every branch is locally one of the
following event equations: white tangency, gray tangency, a wall endpoint,
or the clean-pass boundary of a transparent wall; secondary branches are
their pullbacks through the single white-hit leg.  On circular and flat
pieces these event equations are real analytic away from their declared
root discriminants.

Consequently the global one-step current atlas reduces to:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(FA\arabic*)}]
\item finitely many persistent smooth cores, covered by
Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-section-clean-core-block-current};
\item finitely many compact regular white-tangency charts, covered after
direct/hit assembly by
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-square-root-tangency};
\item regular genuine event faces with nonzero face gradient, carrying the
ordinary coarea current \eqref{eq:clean-core-switching-current};
\item finitely many isolated two-event vertices, after coincident analytic
event arcs have been listed separately.  Every vertex satisfying
\eqref{eq:two-root-transversality} is covered by
Lemma~\ref{lem:two-root-integrability}.
\end{enumerate}
Thus the unresolved geometric part of \textup{(FS1b)} is a finite list of
algebraic strata: degenerate vertices at which the two-event determinant
vanishes, any coincident event arcs, and the exact quotient-chart trace at
the identified wall corners.  For the explicit circular/flat geometry these
are finite algebraic checks.  This
proposition does not perform those determinant checks and does not prove the
propagated positive-envelope or source/tree matching ledgers.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The finite-branch assertion and the primary/secondary description are
\cite[Section~4.1]{StenlundRandom}; the uniform branch count is part of the
same singularity construction.  The free-zone property gives at most one
white collision between returns.  Line--circle, line--flat-wall, and
clean-pass endpoint equations are analytic before a root discriminant
vanishes, and pulling back once through a regular white-hit leg preserves
that property.  Compactness gives finite subcovers of the regular branch
pieces.  The four displayed local results cover all points except branch
endpoints/intersections where the two event gradients fail to be
independent or the fixed quotient chart changes.  Since the original
branch family is finite and analytic on each declared piece, two distinct
pieces have finitely many intersections unless they coincide on an analytic
arc.  This gives exactly the finite residual list stated above.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Corner and lift chart traces cancel on the physical
quotient]
\label{cor:fixed-section-corner-lift-trace}
Equip the fixed section $M$ with Stenlund's identification of the three
candidate values at every wall corner and use torus, rather than lifted
Euclidean, position coordinates.  If test functions are continuous on this
physical quotient, then adjacent wall charts and adjacent lift charts have
the same target trace.  Hence their artificial chart-boundary contributions
to \eqref{eq:clean-core-switching-current} cancel exactly.  Such a chart
boundary contributes no independent positive occurrence mass.  This does
not cancel a genuine grazing return or control a degenerate simultaneous
event approaching the corner.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The corner identification is part of the definition of $F_c$ in
\cite[Section~2.3]{StenlundRandom}: the three conflicting coordinate values
represent one value of the physical return map.  A continuous quotient test
therefore has equal one-sided traces on all three representatives.  A lift
change differs by an integer translation before projection to the torus, so
the two representatives again have identical physical image and test trace.
Insert these equalities into the jump factor of
\eqref{eq:clean-core-switching-current}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Positive-mass local entry/exit DQ and source realization]
\label{cor:fixed-section-local-block-DQ-realization}
There is a compact rectangle
$K_*\Subset\{|y|<1/100,\ |w|<1/100\}$ of positive $\mu$-mass and a parameter
window $|s|<s_*$ on which the $M\to W$ entry branch through the white disk
and its time-reversed $W\to M$ exit branch are jointly analytic, uniformly
non-grazing, and have the clean-core DQ currents
\eqref{eq:clean-core-transport-current}.  Their composition gives the
entry and exit terms $B_0\dot C$ and $\dot B C_0$ in
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-DQ-limit-product-rule} on this core.
Write $K_{\rm ent}=K_*$ and let $K_{\rm exit}\Subset W$ be the corresponding
time-reversed compact exit core.  For $f\in\{c^{\rm br},b^{\rm br}\}$ let
$\lambda_f$ be the fixed smooth block reference on its source chart
$K_f$, let $Y_f$ be its fixed target chart, and set
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-local-core-Banach-pair}
 \mathfrak S_f:=\mathcal M(K_f)_{\rm TV},
 \qquad \mathfrak T_f:=C^1(Y_f).
\end{equation}
Define one linear operator on the whole fixed source space by
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-local-core-source-operator}
 \langle U_f\eta,\phi\rangle
 :=\int_{K_f}D\phi(f_0x)V_f(x)\,d\eta(x),
 \qquad \eta\in\mathfrak S_f,\quad \phi\in\mathfrak T_f.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-local-core-operator-bound}
 \|U_f\|_{\mathfrak S_f\to\mathfrak T_f^*}
 \le\|V_f\|_{L^\infty(K_f)}.
\end{equation}
For every nonzero $h\in C_c^1(K_f)$, $h\ge0$, predeclared as
immutable carrier data before any final test, polynomial, mode, or depth
query, put
$M_{f,h}=\int h\,d\lambda_f$ and
$\nu_{f,h}=M_{f,h}^{-1}h\lambda_f$; it has
$\|\nu_{f,h}\|_{\mathfrak S_f}=1$.
Fix two extra registry indices
$i_{\rm ent},i_{\rm exit}\in\mathcal I_{\rm prop}^{\rm extra}$ and register
the atomic source types $(i_{\rm ent},+)$ and $(i_{\rm exit},+)$ in
$\mathcal T_{\rm src}$; $K_*$ and
$\nu_{f,h}$ are carrier data, not an uncountable occurrence index.  Give each
atom coefficient $M_{f,h}$, carrier $\nu_{f,h}$, operator $U_f$, constant polarity
$+1$, and the predictable envelope
$\mathscr C_{\rm core}:=1+\max_f\|V_f\|_{L^\infty(K_f)}$.  Copy these two
records by the identity occurrence kernel and put $q=\mathscr C_{\rm core}m$.
This is a countable MPD with $\mathscr C_{\rm core}\ge1$; it satisfies the
local exact physical/source $m$-pushforward, scalar-current identity, and
propagated $q$-domination on every restriction of the two tags.  No identity
with the pre-existing complete-tree law is asserted here; the corresponding
physical/tree Radon--Nikodym record remains part of the global
\textup{(FS1c)}--\textup{(FS8)} gate.  A signed smooth $h$ is handled by its
two positive parts only when that decomposition is entered in the MPD
registry.

On these compact analytic cores the frozen maps also satisfy the typed
composition bounds
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-local-core-composition-bounds}
 B_{0,K_{\rm exit}}^*:C^1(M)\longrightarrow C^1(K_{\rm exit}),
 \qquad
 C_0:\mathcal M(K_{\rm ent})\longrightarrow
       \mathcal M(K_{\rm exit}),
\end{equation}
with finite operator norms, where $B_{0,K_{\rm exit}}^*$ is the pullback by
the single analytic exit branch restricted to $K_{\rm exit}$; no global
$C^1(W)$ regularity across billiard singularities is asserted.  Hence the
local terms $B_0\dot C$ and
$\dot B C_0$ are genuine compositions in the same fixed Banach pairs, not
only formal branch symbols.

This is a positive-mass local block-current theorem.  It does not prove the
global direct-block switching atlas, its boundary tightness, or the
hereditary propagated-$q$ estimates required by the global block matching
and recovery gates.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
At $(y,w,s)=(0,0,0)$ the incoming collision is normal, the reflected ray
returns horizontally to the left transparent wall, and both intersections
are transverse.  Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-positive-first-hit-cylinder}
gives a persistent incoming root with strict margins.  The analytic implicit
function theorem applied successively to the incoming collision, reflection,
and transverse exit therefore supplies a neighbourhood on which both branch
maps in the fixed boundary coordinates are analytic.  Choose a compact
rectangle $K_*$ inside it.  It has positive $\mu$-mass by
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-common-law}.  Apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-section-clean-core-block-current} to the entry map and
to the reversed branch.  Formula
\eqref{eq:clean-core-transport-pairing} is exactly
$M_{f,h}\langle U_f\nu_{f,h},\phi\rangle$, and its absolute value is at most
$M_{f,h}\|V_f\|_\infty\|\phi\|_{C^1}$.  Thus the two stated atoms form a typed
positive MPD.  The identity occurrence kernel preserves every record and
integrates the same scalar by construction; multiplication by the fixed
floor $\mathscr C_{\rm core}$ gives $m\le q$ and the claimed local
propagated domination.  Composition with a fixed analytic branch and
pushforward by a fixed branch are bounded respectively on $C^1$ tests and
finite signed measures, proving
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-local-core-composition-bounds}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the block identity does and does not transfer]
\label{rem:fixed-section-block-frontier}
Equation~\eqref{eq:fixed-section-induced-DQ} closes the algebraic
bounded-inducing step for the Stenlund fixed section.  The local first-hit
coarea chart below is the primitive for the $C_c$ block, and the reversed
chart treats $B_c$.  Corollary~\ref{cor:fixed-section-local-block-DQ-realization}
now proves the entry/exit DQ currents and their identity matching on one
positive-mass compact core.  To invoke
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-typed-block-DQ} globally one must still
build the recorded switching atlas in
\eqref{eq:clean-core-switching-current}.  Proposition~
\ref{prop:fixed-section-square-root-tangency} supplies the regular tangency
tightness after direct/hit assembly; one must still prove the propagated-$q$
ledger and cover the direct block together with the simultaneous-event,
and genuine-grazing complement.  Corner and lift chart traces are already
removed by Corollary~\ref{cor:fixed-section-corner-lift-trace}.  Moreover $M^*$ still contains transparent-wall
returns, so this identity does not prove
$\mathrm{SECTION\_FULL\_CM2}$ for the standard full-boundary collision map.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Verified base primitives for the deterministic pilot]
\label{prop:fixed-section-verified-primitives}
Along \eqref{eq:fixed-section-deterministic-path}, the phase section $M$,
the probability $\mu$, the component perimeter vector, and the time-reversal
involution are independent of $s$.  Each $F_{c(s)}$ and every finite
composition of such maps preserve $\mu$.  Consequently:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(V\arabic*)}]
\item the component-mass gauge obstruction
      \eqref{eq:constant-component-mass-vector} vanishes exactly;
\item the unconditioned dynamical carrier change of law has density one;
\item before every stopping, tilt, or occurrence selection, the two-endpoint
      reference is the genuine product law $\mu\otimes\mu$;
\item zero-mass parents may use the fixed null proposal without altering any
      physical current.
\end{enumerate}
These statements verify only the base-law part of the matching and
pair-reference interfaces.  They do not identify a coarea occurrence current
with a source proposal after restriction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Translation leaves both disk perimeters fixed, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-exact-geometry} gives the common section,
common invariant probability, and reversibility.  Invariance is stable under
composition.  Taking two independent copies of this same probability gives
the product law.  The last assertion is the total-kernel convention and
carries zero coefficient and envelope mass.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Positive-mass first-hit cylinder]
\label{prop:fixed-section-positive-first-hit-cylinder}
Use the square lift $[-1/2,1/2]^2$, take the central transparent wall
$\Gamma_L=\{-1/2\}\times[-1/2,1/2]$ with inward normal $e_1$, and use the
explicit path $c(s)=(s,0)$, $|s|<1/400$.  Put
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-explicit-cylinder}
 \mathcal C_*:=\left\{(y,w,s):
 |y|<\frac1{100},\ |w|<\frac1{100},\ |s|<\frac1{400}\right\},
 \quad
 q_0=(-1/2,y),\quad
 u=\frac{(1,w)}{\sqrt{1+w^2}}.
\end{equation}
Every $(y,w,s)\in\mathcal C_*$ represents an interior point of the fixed
section and its next physical collision is the incoming collision with the
white disk in this same torus lift.  The incoming root is the unique root of
$G_x(t,s)=0$ in the fixed interval
\begin{equation}
 I_*=(1/4,1/2),
\label{eq:fixed-section-first-hit-interval}
\end{equation}
whereas the outgoing root is larger than $1/2$.  At the incoming root,
\begin{equation}
 n_x\cdot u<-\frac{99}{100},
 \qquad
 n_x\cdot v_c<-\frac{49}{50}.
\label{eq:fixed-section-cylinder-transversality}
\end{equation}
In particular this is a positive-$(\mu\otimes ds)$-mass clean first-hit
cylinder, not a condition on a possibly empty cell.  More explicitly, its
base-section mass is
\begin{equation}
 \mu(\mathcal C_*|_M)
 =\frac1{\mathcal N_M}\frac1{50}\,
   2\sin\!\left(\arctan\frac1{100}\right)>0.
\label{eq:fixed-section-cylinder-positive-mass}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The unit segment centered at $q_0$ and parallel to $u$ stays at vertical
distance greater than
$1/2-1/100-1/200=97/200>\bar R$ from every lift of a gray-disk centre.
Thus the crossing is a clean pass and lies in the interior of $M$.  The
perpendicular distance $d$ from the line $q_0+\mathbb Ru$ to $c(s)$ obeys
\begin{equation}
 \frac dR\le
 \frac{\frac1{100}+(\frac12+\frac1{400})\frac1{100}}
      {4/25}
 =\frac{601}{6400}.
\label{eq:fixed-section-cylinder-distance}
\end{equation}
Hence the line intersects the white disk.  Writing
$a=(c(s)-q_0)\cdot u$, its two roots are
$t_\pm=a\pm\sqrt{R^2-d^2}$.  The elementary bounds following from
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-explicit-cylinder} give
$1/4<t_-<1/2<t_+$.  Before $t_-$ the vertical distance to every gray centre
is still greater than $97/200>\bar R$, so $t_-$ is the physical first hit,
not merely one of the two algebraic intersections.

At that root
$n_x\cdot u=-\sqrt{1-(d/R)^2}<-99/100$.  Decomposing the incoming normal
in the orthonormal frame $(u,u^\perp)$ and using
$d/R\le601/6400$ and $|w|<1/100$ gives
$n_x\cdot e_1<-49/50$.  Finally $w=\tan\varphi$ on $\Gamma_L$ and
$d\mu=\mathcal N_M^{-1}\cos\varphi\,dy\,d\varphi$, which gives
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-cylinder-positive-mass}; the parameter interval also
has positive length $1/200$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Certified nonzero clean-cell incidence envelope]
\label{cor:fixed-section-clean-incidence-envelope}
On a clean first-hit cell with $|n_x\cdot u|\ge g$, the positive geometric incidence
measure
\[
 dQ_g(x,s):=
 \frac{\mathbf 1_{\rm clean}(x,s)}{2R|n_x(s)\cdot u|}\,d\mu(x)\,ds
\]
is a finite Borel measure on every bounded parameter window and has density at
most $(2Rg)^{-1}$ with respect to $\mu\otimes ds$.  The signed impact-time
mark $a_g=\partial_st_x$ is Borel and satisfies
\[
 |a_g(x,s)|\le |v_c|/g.
\]
Thus the clean one-return graph has an explicit positive envelope and a
uniformly dominated signed geometric mark.  This corollary does not identify
that geometric graph current with the full differentiated transfer-operator
current; that identification remains in \textup{(FS1)}.
For the explicit cylinder $\mathcal C_*$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-positive-first-hit-cylinder}, one may take
$g=99/100$ and the incidence measure has strictly positive mass.  Moreover
its mark has constant sign and
\begin{equation}
 \frac{49}{50}<a_g(x,s)<\frac{100}{99}
 \quad\text{on }\mathcal C_*.
\label{eq:fixed-section-cylinder-mark-bounds}
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The density formula is \eqref{eq:fixed-section-impact-coarea}; the mark bound
follows from \eqref{eq:fixed-section-impact-derivative} and
$|n_x\cdot v_c|\le |v_c|$.
On $\mathcal C_*$ use
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-cylinder-transversality}; both scalar products are
negative, $|n_x\cdot u|\le1$, and the displayed bounds follow.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exact one-return moving-face impact chart]
\label{lem:fixed-section-impact-coarea}
Let $x=(q_0,u)$ be a point of the common fixed section, with $|u|=1$.  Fix a
torus lattice lift on the cell and a Borel interval field
$I_x(s)\subset(0,\infty)$ whose graph is Borel.  Assume that the physical
first collision before the next return is the incoming collision with that
lift of the translated white disk, that it is the unique zero of $G_x$ in
$I_x(s)$, and that $I_x(s)$ contains no other collision time.  Before this
collision $q_0$ and $u$ are independent of $s$.  Put
\begin{equation}
 G_x(t,s):=|q_0+tu-c(s)|^2-R^2.
\label{eq:fixed-section-impact-equation}
\end{equation}
On a clean cell where the selected first-hit root is $t=t_x(s)\in I_x(s)$ and, with
\[
 n_x(s):=R^{-1}\{q_0+t_x(s)u-c(s)\},
\]
one has $n_x(s)\cdot u\le-g<0$, the root and impact point are analytic and
\begin{equation}
 \partial_st_x(s)=
 \frac{n_x(s)\cdot v_c}{n_x(s)\cdot u},
 \qquad
 \partial_s\{q_0+t_x(s)u\}
 =u\frac{n_x(s)\cdot v_c}{n_x(s)\cdot u}.
\label{eq:fixed-section-impact-derivative}
\end{equation}
Moreover, for every bounded Borel test $\phi$ supported in the selected
first-hit set $\{(t,s):t\in I_x(s)\}$,
\begin{equation}
 \iint \phi(t,s)\,\delta_0(G_x(t,s))\,dt\,ds
 =
 \int
 \frac{\phi(t_x(s),s)}
 {2R|n_x(s)\cdot u|}\,ds.
\label{eq:fixed-section-impact-coarea}
\end{equation}
Thus the selected clean one-return incidence graph, its incoming orientation,
and its coarea Jacobian are exact primitive data.  The interval/first-hit and
fixed-lift hypotheses are essential: without them the delta integral sums the
incoming and outgoing roots.  Grazing boxes $|n\cdot u|<g$, branch endpoints,
and lift changes are not estimated by this lemma and must remain on their
recorded deep labels.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
At the root,
$\partial_tG_x=2R(n_x\cdot u)$ and
$\partial_sG_x=-2R(n_x\cdot v_c)$.  The analytic implicit-function theorem
gives \eqref{eq:fixed-section-impact-derivative}.  The one-dimensional
delta/coarea identity restricted to $I_x(s)$ has exactly the selected root and
gives \eqref{eq:fixed-section-impact-coarea}.  The strict incoming inequality
fixes the oriented side.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Uniform local flux entry on the positive cylinder]
\label{prop:fixed-section-local-standard-family-entry}
Shrink the explicit cylinder to the compact box
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-compact-entry-box}
 \mathcal C_{**}:=\left\{(y,w,s):
 |y|\le\frac1{200},\ |w|\le\frac1{200},\quad
 |s|\le\frac1{800}\right\}\Subset\mathcal C_*.
\end{equation}
For each fixed $(y,s)$, push the positive marked incidence
$a_g\,dQ_g$ on the vertical $w$-fibre through the incoming collision map to
the white-disk collision coordinates $(r_+,\varphi_+)$.  After probability
normalization its image is a homogeneous measured unstable curve with
uniform positive length, curvature, and log-density constants.  After a
uniformly bounded finite equal-arclength subdivision (only if required by
the fixed maximum standard-pair length), every $(y,s)$-fibre is a uniform
initial regular standard family.  Each resulting component has length at
least a constant $\ell_{\rm sf}>0$, independent of $(y,s)$, and hence its
boundary functional is at most $\ell_{\rm sf}^{-1}$.

More explicitly, write $\varphi=\arctan w$, $\tau=t_-(y,w,s)$, and
$\cos\varphi_+=-n\cdot u$.  On $\mathcal C_{**}$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-entry-curve-derivatives}
 \left|\frac{dr_+}{d\varphi}\right|
 =\frac{\tau}{\cos\varphi_+}\in\left(\frac14,\frac{50}{99}\right),
 \qquad
 \frac{d\varphi_+}{dr_+}
 =\frac1R+\frac{\cos\varphi_+}{\tau}
 \in\left(\frac{823}{100},\frac{41}{4}\right).
\end{equation}
In particular its length is at least
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-entry-length-floor}
 \ell_*:=\frac12\arctan\frac1{200}>0.
\end{equation}
The unnormalized image density with respect to $dr_+$ is exactly
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-entry-density}
 \rho_{y,s}^{\rm ent}(r_+)
 =\frac1{\mathcal N_M}\frac{\cos\varphi\,a_g}{2R\tau},
\end{equation}
and is bounded above and below with a uniform log-Lipschitz constant.
In particular one may fix
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-local-cone-inclusion}
 \mathcal C_{\rm loc}^u
 :=\left\{8<\frac{d\varphi}{dr}<11\right\},
 \qquad
 \frac{823}{100}<\frac{d\varphi_+}{dr_+}<\frac{41}{4},
\end{equation}
so every entry curve lies compactly inside this one fixed local unstable
cone.  When the local object is propagated under the global
\textup{(FS2)} gate, its recorded common cone is required explicitly to
have bounds $\kappa_-<8<11<\kappa_+$; no later cone inclusion is inferred
from the word ``standard''.  In the parameter convention of
\cite[Definition~4.2]{CanestrariHoles}, take
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-local-family-constants}
 \varpi_*:=1+\sup_{\mathcal C_{**}}
             \operatorname{Lip}_{r_+}(\log\rho_{y,s}^{\rm ent}),
 \quad B_*:=\ell_{\rm sf}^{-1},
 \quad
 D_*:=1+\sup_{\mathcal C_{**}}\|\partial_{r_+}^2\varphi_+\|.
\end{equation}
These finite constants, enlarged once to the convention of the cited
standard-family definition, are independent of $(y,s)$.

The continuum of fibres is kept as a standard-Borel outer
$(y,s)$-occurrence kernel; it is not declared to be one element of the
total-variation completion $\mathfrak S_{\rm sf}^{+}$.  Equivalently, its
phase-space mixture is an initial regular measure in the weak-* sense of
\cite[Definition~5.3 and Lemma~5.8]{CanestrariHoles}, but using that mixture
in the present TV-dominating source space still requires a separate
operator/test extension.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The standard billiard differential formula on a vertical input curve gives
the first identity in
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-entry-curve-derivatives}; the bounds use
$1/4<\tau<1/2$ and $\cos\varphi_+=-n\cdot u>99/100$ from
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-positive-first-hit-cylinder}.  The
curvature formula for a circular target gives the second identity.  Thus the
image lies in one uniform unstable cone, has the length floor
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-entry-length-floor}, and has uniformly bounded
second derivative on the compact box.  Both endpoint angles stay in a fixed
central homogeneity strip.

The total arclength is bounded above on the same compact box.  If that upper
bound exceeds the standard-pair cutoff, divide each curve into the least
number of equal-arclength pieces below the cutoff.  The number of pieces is
uniformly bounded, while the original lower length in
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-entry-length-floor} gives a uniform positive lower
length for every piece.  This proves the stated boundary-functional bound.

Now
$d\mu=\mathcal N_M^{-1}\cos\varphi\,dy\,d\varphi$ and
$dQ_g=(2R\cos\varphi_+)^{-1}d\mu\,ds$.  Changing from $\varphi$ to $r_+$
uses $|d\varphi/dr_+|=\cos\varphi_+/\tau$, so the incidence factor cancels
and yields \eqref{eq:fixed-section-entry-density}.  Every factor there is
positive analytic on the compact box; hence the normalized density has a
uniform log-Lipschitz constant.  These are precisely the local curve,
density, and boundary ingredients in the vertical-line construction of
\cite[Lemmas~5.1--5.2]{CanestrariHoles}.  Keeping $(y,s)$ outside the curve
carrier gives a Borel family of such pairs.  For each fixed $s$ the clean
billiard branch is a diffeomorphism from $(y,\varphi)$ to
$(r_+,\varphi_+)$; Riemann sums in $y$, with the uniform constants above,
converge weak-* to its two-dimensional image measure.  This is the closure
mechanism of the cited Lemma~5.8.  A two-dimensional mixture is
singular with respect to every countable union of curves and therefore
cannot be inserted into the TV-dominating completion
\eqref{eq:standard-family-source-norm}; the weak-* alternative is exactly
the class in the cited definition.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The positive entry box has an unnormalized marked
boundary ledger]
\label{cor:fixed-section-local-aggregate-Z}
On the compact box of
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-local-standard-family-entry}, let
$\eta$ be any finite positive outer occurrence measure in $(y,s)$ and, on
each fibre, choose a component with its original standard-family mass.  Put
\[
 X=1+H+|W_J|^{-1},
\]
where $H$ is the scaled log--H"older density mark of that component.  Then,
for every Borel restriction $A$ of the outer occurrence space,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fixed-section-local-aggregate-Z}
 \int_A X\,d(\eta\ltimes p)
 \le B_{\rm ent}(\eta\ltimes p)(A),
\end{equation}
with one constant $B_{\rm ent}$ independent of $A$.  Hence every
$0<\chi<1$ satisfies the corresponding unnormalized fractional bound
\[
 \int_A X^\chi\,d(\eta\ltimes p)
 \le B_{\rm ent}^\chi(\eta\ltimes p)(A).
\]
This proves the levelwise first-moment part of
Lemma~\ref{lem:global-Lp-levelwise-Z} on the positive entry box without any
rare-cell normalization.  It does not extend the outer kernel to the
TV-dominating source space and does not treat the complementary grazing,
endpoint, or lift-change atlas.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The constants $\varpi_*$ and $B_*$ in
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-local-family-constants} are uniform in $(y,s)$.
Thus on each normalized fibre the component expectation of $X$ is at most
$1+\varpi_*+B_*$.  Integrate this inequality against the original outer measure
restricted to $A$.  Concavity of $x\mapsto x^\chi$ gives the fractional
bound, equivalently by
Lemma~\ref{lem:fractional-family-boundary-mark} for the inverse-length term.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Cellwise regularity criterion for hereditary weighted $Z$]
\label{lem:cellwise-regularity-hereditary-Z}
Let $C$ be any positive-$q$ record cell and let its normalized conditional
source be a standard family.  Let $X_C(a)$ be the full nonnegative $Z$-mark
charged on that cell.  For the bare boundary mark, choosing a component by
its normalized mass gives $X_C=|W_J|^{-1}$ and
$\mathbb E_CX_C=Z(\mathcal G_C)$; any additional recovery, homogeneity,
likelihood, or propagation factor assigned to $Z$ must already be included
in $X_C$ below.  Suppose, uniformly for every global, shallow, and
stopped-deep cell used by the proof,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cellwise-regularity-inputs}
 \mathbb E_C X_C\le B,
 \qquad
 \mathbb E_C\mathscr W^p\le K_p,
 \qquad p>1,\quad 0<\chi p'<1.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cellwise-hereditary-Z-bound}
 \frac1{q(C)}\int_C\mathscr W X_C^\chi\,dq
 \le K_p^{1/p}
 \left(1+\frac{\chi p'B}{1-\chi p'}\right)^{1/p'}.
\end{equation}
Thus a genuinely cellwise first moment of the full $Z$-mark and the
conditional weight-moment input imply the hereditary estimate
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-hereditary-Z}.  A global family-average growth lemma
does not supply the hypotheses in
\eqref{eq:cellwise-regularity-inputs} for rare record cells.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Markov's inequality gives
$\mathbb P_C(X_C>t)\le\min\{1,B/t\}$.  Layer-cake integration with
$q_0=\chi p'<1$ gives
\[
 \mathbb E_CX_C^{q_0}
 \le1+\frac{q_0B}{1-q_0}.
\]
Apply H\"older with exponents $(p,p')$ to
$\mathscr W X_C^\chi$.  This proves
\eqref{eq:cellwise-hereditary-Z-bound}.  Multiplication by $q(C)$ gives the
unnormalized hereditary form.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Average-$Z$ obstruction to hereditary conditioning]
\label{prop:average-Z-not-hereditary}
Fix the maximum admissible standard-pair length $\delta_{\rm sf}>0$ and any
admissible long-curve length $0<\ell_0\le\delta_{\rm sf}$.  For every
$0<\epsilon<\min\{\ell_0,1/2\}$ there is a regular two-component standard
family whose densities and curvatures are uniformly regular and whose global
boundary functional is at most $\ell_0^{-1}+1$, but which has a record cell
$C_\epsilon$
with
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:average-Z-hereditary-countermodel}
 \frac1{q(C_\epsilon)}
 \int_{C_\epsilon}Z^\chi\,dq=\epsilon^{-\chi}.
\end{equation}
Consequently the Growth Lemma and its family-average consequences in
\cite[Lemma~6.14 and Corollary~6.15]{CanestrariHoles} cannot by themselves
prove the hereditary route \textup{(FS3a)}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Take two straight homogeneous curves of lengths $\ell_0$ and $\epsilon$, with
component probabilities $1-\epsilon^2$ and $\epsilon^2$ and constant
normalized densities.  Their global boundary functional is
$(1-\epsilon^2)/\ell_0+\epsilon\le\ell_0^{-1}+1$.  Let the immutable record
cell select the
short component.  Its normalized family consists of that curve alone, so
its $Z$ is $\epsilon^{-1}$ and
\eqref{eq:average-Z-hereditary-countermodel} follows.  Curvature and density
regularity are independent of $\epsilon$.
\end{proof}

The following package is deliberately finite in the number of \emph{types}
of assertion but uniform over every depth.  A finite computation at a few
values of $N$ is not a substitute for any of its quantified clauses.

\begin{definition}[Fixed-section certification package
$\mathrm{FS\_CERT}(d_Z)$]
\label{def:fixed-section-cert}
For the rational model \eqref{eq:fixed-section-rational-data}, require one
query-independent incidence tree and the following record-compatible data.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(FS\arabic*)}]
\item On each clean moving-white collision face, the impact equation and its
coarea map define the exact physical occurrence mass $m$, the dominating
positive envelope $q$, and the signed derivative current before any
frequency, mode, or shallow/deep query is made.  Grazing and endpoint boxes
are assigned to the same immutable deep record rather than discarded.
This consists of three separately falsifiable clauses:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(FS1\alph*)}]
\item $\mathrm{BLOCK\_DQ}_{M,W}$ holds, including the test-side strong
continuity \eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-test-continuity};
\item on every fixed atlas depth the genuine grazing, endpoint, and
lift-change currents satisfy the common-atlas BL$^*$ convergence and
boundary tightness of \eqref{eq:face-DQ-weak-convergence}--
\eqref{eq:face-DQ-boundary-tightness}, while artificial direct/entry faces
are removed only by the trace identity
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-tangency-trace-identity}.  Proposition~
\ref{prop:fixed-section-square-root-tangency} verifies the required
integrability and tightness on every compact regular tangency subatlas; the
positive current envelope and exact local diagonal match are
Corollary~\ref{cor:fixed-section-tangency-positive-envelope}; the
finite reduction in
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-finite-event-atlas} leaves only its
listed degenerate multi-event strata in this clause; the quotient-corner and
lift traces are removed by
Corollary~\ref{cor:fixed-section-corner-lift-trace};
\item the query-independent MPD and physical envelope satisfy
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-physical-envelope}, so
Corollary~\ref{cor:fixed-section-diagonal-block-match} constructs the exact
block occurrence/source bridge.  In particular it verifies the certificate
\[
 \mathrm{BLOCK\_MATCH}_{M,W}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
The direct, entry, and exit currents are then assembled by
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-DQ-limit-product-rule}; no derivative of a
parameter-dependent inducing time or intermediate $W$-centering is inserted.
\item The oriented face push-forward is a positive flux standard family
with a uniform long-side lower bound, distortion mark, recovery time, and
boundary recursion.  The failed long-side part satisfies the recorded deep
tail on the same occurrence law.  Proposition~
\ref{prop:fixed-section-local-standard-family-entry} verifies this statement
only for the compact positive entry box and keeps its continuum of fibres as
an outer occurrence kernel; the complement remains part of this clause.
\item Put all recovery, boundary, and homogeneity costs into $Z$.  There are
$\chi_Z>0$ and $K_Z<\infty$, and one of the following two mutually
compatible certification routes is used on the same immutable occurrence
tree.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(FS3\alph*)}]
\item In the hereditary route, every global, shallow, or stopped-deep record
cell $C$ obeys
\begin{equation}
 \int_C W(a)Z(a)^{\chi_Z}\,q(da)
 \le K_Z q(C).
\label{eq:fixed-section-hereditary-Z}
\end{equation}
The weight $W$ contains every propagation, carrier, likelihood, and local
PPE mark charged by the active handler.  One sufficient route is the
cellwise first moment of the complete $Z$-mark and the conditional
weight-moment criterion \eqref{eq:cellwise-regularity-inputs}; regular-family
boundary control gives only its inverse-length part.  A global family-average
$Z$ recursion is explicitly insufficient by
Proposition~\ref{prop:average-Z-not-hereditary}.
\item In the aggregate route, the global coarea law satisfies
\eqref{eq:translation-global-Z-moment}, the shallow and complete deep owner
families have bounded overlap already charged in $W$, and their original
unnormalized masses satisfy
\eqref{eq:translation-aggregate-shallow-Z-moment}--
\eqref{eq:translation-aggregate-deep-Z-moment}.  For these two finite-$s$
aggregate estimates, it is enough to verify the single level ledger
\eqref{eq:unnormalized-level-ledger} with a strict post-mark tail gap, as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:unnormalized-level-ledger}.  A more primitive sufficient
route is the unweighted depth tail, the levelwise first moment of the full
$Z$-mark, and the one global $L^p$ weight bound in
Lemma~\ref{lem:global-Lp-levelwise-Z}; for the inverse-length part the first
moment is exactly the measured-family boundary estimate in
Lemma~\ref{lem:fractional-family-boundary-mark}.  This route never divides by the
mass of a rare owner and therefore asks for no hereditary conditional
moment.
\end{enumerate}
\item The source tree is the push-forward of that same physical occurrence
tree.  On every record restriction, the coefficient, $m$, $q$, carrier,
owner, clock, and scalar current agree exactly.  Thus
$\mathrm{PROP\_Q\_MATCH}$ is constructed, including zero-mass parents,
rather than assumed after selection.
\item After the stopped renewal at
$j_- =\lceil(1-\theta_1)N\rceil$, the terminal magnet entrance pieces carry
one invariant native-$z$ jet cone.  For every $N$ and every admissible frozen
context it supplies the component count, rough-transfer bound, remainder
bound, and nonvanishing $D=d_Z+1$ jet required by
Definition~\ref{def:translation-ppe-terminal}.  A sufficient noncircular
route to the last item is the exact affine recurrence, invariant oriented
cone, and terminal seed of
Proposition~\ref{prop:translation-terminal-jet-cone}; isolated nonzero jets
at finitely many depths are not substituted.  Proposition~
\ref{prop:fixed-section-exact-qnl-witness} supplies the required nonzero
geometric seed on an actual pilot return branch, and Corollary~
\ref{cor:fixed-section-pilot-finite-type-gate} upgrades it to a
parameter-uniform absolutely continuous degree-one sublevel gate.  These
results do not by themselves supply the invariant continuation cone, the
exact endpoint-slope identification, or rough transfer from all stopped
parents.  Alternatively, Theorem~
\ref{thm:anchored-growing-alphabet-transfer} shows that it is enough to prove
one uniform anchored MNL estimate on the finite full-branch truncations;
their excluded countable tail is then automatically exponentially small at
the prescribed depth.
\item The retained amplitude obeys the normalized weighted sublevel and
conditional mark-moment inequalities in the precise clause \textup{(PPE2)}
of Definition~\ref{def:physical-legal-extension}.  Buffer
continuation and finite-frequency uniformity give \textup{(PPE3)--(PPE4)}
on the identical stopped tree.  One sufficient route to \textup{(PPE2)} is
\eqref{eq:translation-weighted-bad-mass}, the finite-operation registry
\eqref{eq:translation-PPE-word-registry}, and the conditional Lyapunov drift
\eqref{eq:translation-PPE-Lyapunov-drift}; \textup{(PPE4)} additionally
requires parameter-uniform cone and moving-cut bounds up to $L(s)$, in view
of Proposition~\ref{prop:translation-PPE4-parameter-countermodel}.
\item The canonical depth
\begin{equation}
 \ell_{\rm fs}(a)=\max\left\{\ell_{\rm sym}(a),
 \left\lceil\gamma_Z^{-1}\log^+Z(a)\right\rceil,
 R_{\rm entry}(a)\right\}
\label{eq:fixed-section-canonical-depth}
\end{equation}
is frozen before all queries.  Its shallow/deep split agrees with the face,
word, and recovery handlers and proves both required inclusions between
word-depth and recovery rank.
\item \begin{flushleft}Every hypothesis of the fixed-phase assembly
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-phase-cm2-assembly}, equivalently every interface in
\textup{(H1)--(H5)} of Theorem~\ref{thm:global-cm2-assembly}, not
literally supplied by \textup{(FS1)--(FS7)} holds on this same tree.  This
includes the complete \textup{(H1)} moment/refinement package and
\textup{(H2)} coupling package; $\mathrm{CEM\_DOM}$,
$\mathrm{TENERGY}_{W}$, $\mathrm{DUAL\_MASS\_KERNEL}$, $\mathrm{MPD}$,
$\mathrm{POST\_CARRIER\_DOM}$, $\mathrm{PHYS\_TEST\_LIFT}$,
$\mathrm{MAIN\_CLOCK\_COVERAGE}$, $\mathrm{SEL\_JSL}$,
$\mathrm{CLOCK\_PROB}_{W}$, $\mathrm{CLOCK\_SRC}_{W}$,
$\mathrm{KRH}_{p_W}$, $\mathrm{USC}_{\rm end}^{W}$, and
$\mathrm{BR}_{\rm sec}$; as well as $\mathrm{QT}_{\rm path}$,
$\mathrm{UCR}_{\rm sh}$, $\mathrm{FZ}_{\rm geom}$,
$\mathrm{SF}_{\rm split}$, $\mathrm{CP}_{\rm env}$, and the complete
fixed-core/boundary part of $\mathrm{MT}_{\rm DQ}$.
All these operators, currents, tests, and norms are instantiated directly for
the fixed-section maps $F_{c(s)}$; this clause does not identify them with the
standard full-boundary collision-map target.
\end{flushleft}
\item All rate variables lie in explicit rational intervals on which the
direct or terminal PPE window, the recovery window, and every final CM2
minimum are strictly positive.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[A hereditary estimate gives the three normalized laws]
\label{lem:fixed-section-hereditary-Z}
Clauses \textup{(FS3a)} and \textup{(FS4)} imply the global, normalized
shallow, and normalized stopped-deep $Z$-moments required in
Proposition~\ref{prop:translation-Z-closes-recovery}, with the same constant
$K_Z$ whenever the normalizing mass is positive.  Clause \textup{(FS4)}
separately makes a zero-mass cell contribute the fixed null proposal and zero
current; this conclusion is not a consequence of \textup{(FS3a)} alone.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Take $C$ to be the whole occurrence space for the global law.  For a
positive-$q$-mass shallow or stopped-deep cell, divide
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-hereditary-Z} by $q(C)$.  This is precisely the
corresponding $q$-normalized moment of $WZ^{\chi_Z}$.  No inference from an
unconditional average to a rare conditional cell is used.  The zero-mass
proposal/current statement is exactly the separate \textup{(FS4)} clause.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The aggregate estimate gives the unnormalized recovery laws]
\label{lem:fixed-section-aggregate-Z}
Clauses \textup{(FS3b)} and \textup{(FS4)} imply
$\mathrm{TR\_Z\_AREC}$ and hence the aggregate shallow/deep recovery
estimates through
Proposition~\ref{prop:translation-aggregate-Z-closes-recovery}.  Zero-mass
owners again contribute the fixed null proposal and zero current by
\textup{(FS4)}, not by a hidden normalization convention.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Clause \textup{(FS3b)} is stated on the same global occurrence law and the
same shallow/deep owner restrictions as
Definition~\ref{def:translation-Z-aggregate-recovery}.  Bounded overlap is
paid in the immutable mark, so positive restriction and
Lemma~\ref{lem:unnormalized-level-ledger} give the two aggregate moments.
Clause \textup{(FS4)} identifies these restrictions with the actual source
owners and supplies the null proposal on a zero-mass owner.  No ownerwise
division is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional fixed-section completion theorem for the deterministic pilot]
\label{thm:fixed-section-nonvacuity-certificate}
Assume $\mathrm{FS\_CERT}(d_Z)$ for the deterministic path
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-deterministic-path} in the explicit rational model
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-rational-data}.  Then the exact product/source law
and the prescribed-depth PPE give $\mathrm{PAIR\_TENERGY}$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:ppe-implies-pair-tenergy}; clauses
\textup{(FS1)--(FS7)} give the translation face, matching, recovery, and
shallow/deep gates; and \textup{(FS8)--(FS9)} close the remaining fixed-phase
ledger.  Consequently Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-phase-cm2-assembly} applies and
the deterministic maps $F_{c(s)}$ satisfy the fixed-section CM2 conclusion.
This is not a theorem about the standard full-boundary collision map.

At present the exact pilot geometry and its QNL witness are proved in
Propositions~\ref{prop:fixed-section-exact-geometry} and
\ref{prop:fixed-section-exact-qnl-witness}; the persistent local
finite-type gate is Corollary~
\ref{cor:fixed-section-pilot-finite-type-gate}.  The inducing and block-current
results are Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-two-step-inducing},
Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-typed-block-DQ}, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-section-clean-core-block-current}, together with
Corollaries~\ref{cor:fixed-section-tangency-trace-cancellation} and
\ref{cor:fixed-section-local-block-DQ-realization}.  The regular global-atlas
reduction is supplied by Proposition~
\ref{prop:fixed-section-square-root-tangency}, Corollary~
\ref{cor:fixed-section-tangency-positive-envelope}, Proposition~
\ref{prop:fixed-section-finite-event-atlas}, and Corollary~
\ref{cor:fixed-section-corner-lift-trace}.  The positive entry construction
is Proposition~\ref{prop:fixed-section-verified-primitives}, Proposition~
\ref{prop:fixed-section-positive-first-hit-cylinder}, Lemma~
\ref{lem:fixed-section-impact-coarea}, Corollary~
\ref{cor:fixed-section-clean-incidence-envelope}, and Proposition~
\ref{prop:fixed-section-local-standard-family-entry}.  The conditional
cellwise bridge is Lemma~\ref{lem:cellwise-regularity-hereditary-Z}.  The alternative
aggregate bridge is reduced by
Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-section-aggregate-Z} to one depth-stratified marked
occurrence ledger, but that concrete ledger has not yet been certified on
the complement of the positive entry box.  The remaining
all-depth parts of \textup{(FS1)--(FS9)} are a certification target.  In
particular the theorem is a checkable implication, not a claim that the
global block atlas, terminal jet, global recordwise matching, or either
recovery ledger has already been certified.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Height-two Kac centering and block resolvent]
\label{prop:height-two-kac-block-resolvent}
Let a common height-two phase extension have base map $F$, return indicator
$E$, roof $r=1+\one_E$, and mean $\bar r=\mu(r)$.  For an observable $h$ on
the phase extension define
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:height-two-induced-observable}
 \mathcal S h(x):=h(x,0)+\one_E(x)h(x,1).
\end{equation}
Then its invariant probability and exact induced centering obey
\begin{align}
 \widehat\mu(h)&=\frac{\mu(\mathcal S h)}{\bar r},
 \label{eq:height-two-kac-measure}\\
 \mathcal S(h-\widehat\mu(h))
 &=\mathcal S h-r\widehat\mu(h)
  =\mathcal S h-\mu(\mathcal S h)
    +(\bar r-r)\widehat\mu(h).
 \label{eq:height-two-centering-correction}
\end{align}
Thus bounded roof leaves a genuine centered roof correction.

For a parameterized family in one common gauge, let $h_s$ be a moving
observable and let a dot denote the derivative at $s=0$.  Whenever the four
terms below exist in the declared source/test dual, differentiation gives
the exact identity
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:height-two-differentiated-kac-observable}
 \left.\partial_s\{\mathcal S_sh_s
       -r_s\widehat\mu_s(h_s)\}\right|_{s=0}
 =\dot{\mathcal S}h+\mathcal S\dot h
  -\dot r\,\widehat\mu(h)
  -r\left.\partial_s\widehat\mu_s(h_s)\right|_{s=0}.
\end{equation}
Here $\dot{\mathcal S}h$ includes the moving-level and return-partition
boundary current, and the last derivative is the total derivative of the
scalar $\widehat\mu_s(h_s)$.  In particular $\dot r$ is not replaced by an
ordinary bounded observable merely because $r_s\in\{1,2\}$.

If the transfer operator on $M\sqcup W$ is
$\widehat P=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\C&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$, put
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:height-two-return-polynomial}
 R(z):=zA+z^2BC,\qquad D(z):=I-R(z).
\end{equation}
Whenever the displayed inverses exist,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:height-two-block-resolvent}
 (I-z\widehat P)^{-1}
 =\begin{pmatrix}
 D(z)^{-1}&zD(z)^{-1}B\\
 zCD(z)^{-1}&I+z^2CD(z)^{-1}B
 \end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first identity is Kac's formula on the two tower levels.  Since
$\mu(\mathcal S h)=\bar r\widehat\mu(h)$, adding and subtracting this scalar
gives \eqref{eq:height-two-centering-correction}.  The resolvent formula is
the Schur-complement inverse of
$\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I-zA&-zB\\-zC&I\end{smallmatrix}\right)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A bounded roof does not transfer CM2]
\label{prop:bounded-roof-not-CM2}
There is a constant-roof-two extension whose induced base map is mixing but
which has a nondecaying centered phase correlation.  Hence base CM2 and a
uniform roof bound do not imply phase CM2.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $S$ be any mixing probability map and define
$\widehat T(x,0)=(x,1)$, $\widehat T(x,1)=(Sx,0)$.  The base return map is
$S$, whereas $\phi(x,\ell)=(-1)^\ell$ is centered and satisfies
$\phi\circ\widehat T=-\phi$.  Its correlation has absolute value one at
every time.  On the other hand
$\mathcal S\phi=0$, so the induced observable does not see this phase mode.
In \eqref{eq:height-two-return-polynomial} this obstruction is the
noninvertibility of $D(-1)=I-P$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Height-two Wiener phase-lift criterion]
\label{thm:finite-return-Wiener-CM2}
Assume the height-two phase extension is typed on fixed Banach source/test
pairs and satisfies the exact Kac and block identities above.  Assume the
raw currents $\dot A,\dot B,\dot C$ exist with the block test continuity in
\eqref{eq:fixed-section-block-test-continuity}, so that
$\dot{\widehat P}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\dot A&\dot B\\
\dot C&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$ is a typed raw phase current.  Assemble
the moving return-partition current and the differentiated Kac centering,
and denote the resulting complete centered current by
\[
 \widehat K:\widehat{\mathfrak B}_2^{\circ}
 \longrightarrow(\widehat{\mathfrak T}^{\circ})^*.
\]
Put $\widehat\Pi h=\widehat\mu(h)\one$ and
$\widehat Q=\widehat P-\widehat\Pi$.  On the declared centered source and
test spaces write
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:height-two-left-right-resolvents}
 \widehat R_{\rm src}^{\circ}(w)
 :=\sum_{m\ge0}w^m\widehat Q^m,
 \qquad
 \widehat R_{\rm test}^{\circ}(z)
 :=\sum_{n\ge0}z^n(\widehat Q^*)^n.
\end{equation}
Suppose:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(RW\arabic*)}]
\item writing $\Pi_Mh:=\mu(h)\one$ for the base invariant projection, the
      base source-space singular coefficient is normalized by the mean roof,
      \[
       D(z)^{-1}=\frac{\Pi_M}{\bar r(1-z)}+H_{\rm src}(z),
      \]
      with the analogous test-side formula; after the prefix--suffix blocks
      in \eqref{eq:height-two-block-resolvent} are inserted, their full phase
      singular coefficient is exactly $\widehat\Pi/(1-z)$.  The remainders
      generated by $H_{\rm src}$ and its test counterpart belong to their
      exponentially weighted operator Wiener algebras;
\item $D(z)$ is invertible for $|z|=1$, $z\ne1$ (return-phase
      aperiodicity);
\item $B,C$, their test actions, the observable lift
      \eqref{eq:height-two-induced-observable}, the roof correction
      \eqref{eq:height-two-centering-correction}, every term of the
      differentiated Kac identity
      \eqref{eq:height-two-differentiated-kac-observable}, and all three
      block currents, including the assembly defining $\widehat K$, act
      boundedly on the stated source/test Wiener pairs.
\end{enumerate}
Then both resolvents in
\eqref{eq:height-two-left-right-resolvents} have exponentially absolutely
summable coefficients.  Moreover the bilinear current-valued function
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:height-two-double-Wiener-response}
 \mathcal G_{\widehat K}(z,w)[g,f]
 :=\left\langle
  \widehat K\widehat R_{\rm src}^{\circ}(w)g,
  \widehat R_{\rm test}^{\circ}(z)f
 \right\rangle
\end{equation}
belongs to the corresponding two-variable Wiener algebra, and hence
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:height-two-absolute-CM2}
 \sum_{m,n\ge0}
 \left|\left\langle
  \widehat K\widehat Q^mg,(\widehat Q^*)^nf
 \right\rangle\right|<\infty
\end{equation}
for the declared phase tests.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Insert the centered inverse of $D(z)$ into
\eqref{eq:height-two-block-resolvent}.  The weighted Wiener algebra is closed
under finite block sums and bounded multiplication, so the centered source
resolvent and its test adjoint belong to the two declared one-variable
algebras.  Pair their coefficients through the bounded bilinear current
$\widehat K$; the projective product estimate for the two Wiener norms gives
\eqref{eq:height-two-double-Wiener-response}.  Its $(n,m)$ coefficient is
exactly
$\langle\widehat K\widehat Q^mg,(\widehat Q^*)^nf\rangle$, so its coefficient
norm is the sum in \eqref{eq:height-two-absolute-CM2}.  No ill-typed left
composition of a source resolvent with a test-dual current is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Response transfer between sections]
\label{def:section-full-response-transfer}
Write
\[
 \mathrm{SECTION\_FULL\_CM2}
\]
for the conjunction of the following five explicit gates, not a catch-all
hypothesis.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(SF\arabic*)}]
\item \textup{COMMON\_REFINEMENT}: one fixed phase refinement contains both
the Stenlund section $M$ and the solid-boundary section $N=G\sqcup W$, and
$F_{c(s)}$ and $T_{c(s)}$ are their first-return maps with uniformly bounded,
recorded Borel return-word partitions;
\item \textup{RETURN\_BLOCK\_DQ}: every return block, including moving
partition boundaries, satisfies the typed current theorem and exact
occurrence/source matching;
\item \textup{KAC\_CENTERING}: observables, invariant probabilities, and
their differentiated centerings obey the analogue of
\eqref{eq:height-two-centering-correction} and the full four-term identity
\eqref{eq:height-two-differentiated-kac-observable}; in particular
$\dot{\mathcal S}h$, $\mathcal S\dot h$, $\dot r\,\widehat\mu(h)$, and
$r\,\partial_s[\widehat\mu_s(h_s)]|_0$ are separately typed, and $\dot r$
is kept as a boundary current;
\item \textup{RETURN\_WIENER\_APERIODICITY}: on every finite return-word
phase matrix, removal of the Kac invariant pole leaves centered source and
test resolvents in compatible weighted operator Wiener algebras, with no
nontrivial unit-circle return resonance.  For a height-two matrix this is
exactly \textup{(RW1)--(RW3)}; the general finite matrix statement is an
additional gate, not a consequence of the height-two formula;
\item \textup{PHASE\_TEST\_NORM\_LIFT}: the physical observables, block
currents, all differentiated Kac/roof terms, and the two CM2 norms are
boundedly intertwined on the same phase Banach pairs.
\end{enumerate}
Only under these gates may the preceding fixed-section theorem be transferred
to the original full collision-map target.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[The remaining object bridge]
\label{rem:fixed-section-object-bridge}
The Stenlund map $F_c$ records gray-disk collisions and selected transparent
wall crossings, while a white-disk collision is not a return.  It is therefore
not the standard collision map on
